= {\n    34→    0: \"circle1\",\n    35→    1: \"circle2\",\n    36→    2: \"leaf1\",\n    37→    3: \"leaf2\",\n    38→    4: \"leaf3\",\n    39→    5: \"leaf1_swap1\",\n    40→    6: \"leaf1_swap2\",\n    41→}\n    42→\n    43→OUTPUT_STIMULI = [\n    44→    \"circle1\",\n    45→    \"circle2\",\n    46→    \"circle3\",\n    47→    \"leaf1\",\n    48→    \"leaf2\",\n    49→    \"leaf3\",\n    50→    \"leaf1_swap1\",\n    51→    \"leaf1_swap2\",\n    52→]\n    53→OUTPUT_STIM_TO_IDX = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(OUTPUT_STIMULI)}\n    54→\n    55→BRAIN_REGIONS = [\"V1\", \"mHV\", \"lHV\", \"aHV\", \"other\"]\n    56→\n    57→\n    58→@dataclass\n    59→class BehaviorView:\n    60→    exp_type: str\n    61→    file_name: str\n    62→    full_key: str\n    63→    beh: argparse.Namespace:\n    79→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Convert imaging data into decoder format.\")\n    80→    parser.add_argument(\"outpicklefile\", type=str, help=\"Output pickle file path.\")\n    81→    parser.add_argument(\"--full\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Process all sessions (default behavior).\")\n    82→    parser.add_argument(\"--sample\", -> str:\n    92→    parts = full_key.split(\"_\")\n    93→    if len(parts) < 5:\n    94→        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected session key format: {full_key}\")\n    95→    return \"_\".join(parts[:5])\n    96→\n    97→\n    98→def parse_date_and_block(raw_key: str) -> tuple[dt.date, int]:\n    99→    parts = raw_key.split(\"_\")\n   100→    if len(parts) != 5:\n   101→        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected raw key format: {raw_key}\")\n   102→    date = dt.date(int(parts[1]), int(parts[2]), int(parts[3]))\n   103→    block = int(parts[4])\n   104→    return date, block\n   105→\n   106→\n   107→def load_exp_info() -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:\n   108→    return np.load(EXP_INFO_PATH, allow_pickle=True).item()\n   109→\n   110→\n   111→def iter_behavior_files() -> list[Path]:\n   112→    files = []\n   113→    for path in sorted(BEH_DIR.glob(\"Beh_*.npy\")):\n   114→        if path.name in {\n   115→            \"Beh_no_pretrain.npy\",\n   116→            \"Beh_pretrain_on_grat_image.npy\",\n   117→            \"Beh_pretrain_on_nat_image.npy\",\n   118→        }:\n   119→            continue\n   120→        files.append(path)\n   121→    return files\n   122→\n   123→\n   124→def arrays_match(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray) -> bool:\n   125→    if a.dtype.kind in {\"U\", \"S\", \"O\"} or b.dtype.kind in {\"U\", \"S\", \"O\"}:\n   126→        return np.array_equal(a, b)\n   127→    return np.allclose(a, b, equal_nan=True)\n   128→\n   129→\n   130→def validate_duplicate_behavior_views(raw_key: str, views: list[BehaviorView]) -> None:\n   131→    if len(views) < 2:\n   132→        return\n   133→\n   134→    ref = views[0].beh\n   135→    keys_to_match = [\n   136→        \"WallName\",\n   137→        \"SoundPos\",\n   138→        \"StartFr\",\n   139→        \"EndFr\",\n   140→        \"isRew\",\n   141→        \"SoundFr\",\n   142→        \"RewardFr\",\n   143→        \"ft_trInd\",\n   144→        \"ft_CorrSpc\",\n   145→        \"ft_Pos\",\n   146→        \"ft_RunSpeed\",\n   147→    ]\n   148→    for view in views[1:]:\n   149→        for key in keys_to_match:\n   150→            if not arrays_match(np.asarray(ref[key]), np.asarray(view.beh[key])):\n   151→                raise ValueError(\n   152→                    f\"Duplicate behavior views disagree for {raw_key}: \"\n   153→                    f\"{views[0].file_name}/{views[0].full_key} vs {view.file_name}/{view.full_key} on {key}\"\n   154→                )\n   155→\n   156→\n   157→def build_wall_to_stimulus_map(raw_key: str, views: list[BehaviorView]) -> dict[str, str]:\n   158→    label_map: dict[str, str] = {}\n   159→\n   160→    for view in views:\n   161→        uniq_walls = list(map(str, np.asarray(view.beh[\"UniqWalls\"])))\n   162→        stim_ids = np.asarray(view.beh[\"stim_id\"])\n   163→        for wall, stim_id in zip(uniq_walls, stim_ids):\n   164→            if np.isnan(stim_id):\n   165→                continue\n   166→            canonical = CANONICAL_STIM_BY_ID[int(stim_id)]\n   167→            prev = label_map.get(wall)\n   168→            if prev is not None and prev != canonical:\n   169→                raise ValueError(\n   170→                    f\"Conflicting stimulus mapping inside raw session {raw_key}: {wall} -> {prev} vs {canonical}\"\n   171→                )\n   172→            label_map[wall] = canonical\n   173→\n   174→    present_walls = set(map(str, np.unique(views[0].beh[\"WallName\"])))\n   175→    missing = sorted(present_walls - set(label_map))\n   176→    for wall in list(missing):\n   177→        if wall in OUTPUT_STIMULI:\n   178→            label_map[wall] = wall\n   179→    missing = sorted(present_walls - set(label_map))\n   180→    if missing:\n   181→        raise ValueError(f\"Missing label mapping for session {raw_key}: {missing}\")\n   182→\n   183→    return label_map\n   184→\n   185→\n   186→def choose_behavior_reference(views: list[BehaviorView]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n   187→    def score(view: BehaviorView) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n   188→        non_nan = int(np.count_nonzero(~np.isnan(np.asarray(view.beh[\"stim_id\"], dtype=float))))\n   189→        return (non_nan, view.file_name, view.full_key)\n   190→\n   191→    return max(views, key=score).beh\n   192→\n   193→\n   194→def build_session_specs() -> list[SessionSpec]:\n   195→    exp_info = load_exp_info()\n   196→    raw_to_subject: dict[str, str] = {}\n   197→    for records in exp_info.values():\n   198→        for rec in records:\n   199→            raw_key = f\"{rec['mname']}_{rec['datexp']}_{rec['blk']}\"\n   200→            raw_to_subject[raw_key] = rec[\"mname\"]\n   201→\n   202→    raw_to_views: dict[str, list[BehaviorView]] = defaultdict(list)\n   203→    for beh_path in iter_behavior_files():\n   204→        exp_type = beh_path.stem.removeprefix(\"Beh_\")\n   205→        beh_all = np.load(beh_path, allow_pickle=True).item()\n   206→        for full_key, beh in beh_all.items():\n   207→            raw_key = parse_raw_session_key(full_key)\n   208→            raw_to_views[raw_key].append(\n   209→                BehaviorView(exp_type=exp_type, file_name=beh_path.name, full_key=full_key, beh=beh)\n   210→            )\n   211→\n   212→    subject_dates: dict[str, list[tuple[dt.date, int]]] = defaultdict(list)\n   213→    raw_date_block: dict[str, tuple[dt.date, int]] = {}\n   214→    for raw_key in raw_to_views:\n   215→        date, block = parse_date_and_block(raw_key)\n   216→        raw_date_block[raw_key] = (date, block)\n   217→        subject_dates[raw_to_subject[raw_key]].append((date, block))\n   218→\n   219→    subject_first_date = {\n   220→        subject: min(date for date, _ in date_blocks)\n   221→        for subject, date_blocks in subject_dates.items()\n   222→    }\n   223→\n   224→    specs: list[SessionSpec] = []\n   225→    for raw_key in sorted(raw_to_views):\n   226→        views = raw_to_views[raw_key]\n   227→        validate_duplicate_behavior_views(raw_key, views)\n   228→        subject = raw_to_subject[raw_key]\n   229→        date, block = raw_date_block[raw_key]\n   230→        training_day = float((date - subject_first_date[subject]).days) + 0.01 * (block - 1)\n   231→        specs.append(\n   232→            SessionSpec(\n   233→                raw_key=raw_key,\n   234→                subject=subject,\n   235→                date=date,\n   236→                block=block,\n   237→                behavior_views=views,\n   238→                behavior_ref=choose_behavior_reference(views),\n   239→                wall_to_stimulus=build_wall_to_stimulus_map(raw_key, views),\n   240→                training_day=training_day,\n   241→            )\n   242→        )\n   243→\n   244→    return specs\n   245→\n   246→\n   247→def build_brain_region_idx(iarea: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   248→    idx = np.full(iarea.shape, 4, dtype=np.int8)\n   249→    idx[iarea == 8] = 0\n   250→    idx[np.isin(iarea, [0, 1, 2, 9])] = 1\n   251→    idx[np.isin(iarea, [5, 6])] = 2\n   252→    idx[np.isin(iarea, [3, 4])] = 3\n   253→    return idx\n   254→\n   255→\n   256→def get_trial_frame_indices(beh: dict[str, Any], nframes: int) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n   257→    ft_trind = np.asarray(beh[\"ft_trInd\"][:nframes])\n   258→    ft_corr = np.asarray(beh[\"ft_CorrSpc\"][:nframes], dtype=bool)\n   259→    ft_move = np.asarray(beh[\"ft_move\"][:nframes]) > 0\n   260→    ntrials = int(beh[\"ntrials\"])\n   261→    frame_sets: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   262→\n   263→    for trial in range(ntrials):\n   264→        idx = np.flatnonzero((ft_trind == trial) & ft_corr & ft_move)\n   265→        if idx.size == 0:\n   266→            frame_sets.append(idx)\n   267→            continue\n   268→        frame_sets.append(idx.astype(np.int64, copy=False))\n   269→\n   270→    return frame_sets\n   271→\n   272→\n   273→def load_spike_planes(raw_key: str) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n   274→    path = SPK_DIR / f\"{raw_key}_neural_data.npy\"\n   275→    obj = np.load(path, allow_pickle=True).item()\n   276→    return obj[\"spks\"]\n   277→\n   278→\n   279→def load_retinotopy(raw_key: str) -> np.ndarray:\n   280→    mouse_date = \"_\".join(raw_key.split(\"_\")[:4])\n   281→    path = RETINO_DIR / f\"{mouse_date}_trans.npz\"\n   282→    return np.load(path, allow_pickle=True)[\"iarea\"]\n   283→\n   284→\n   285→def position_to_bins(ft_pos_dm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   286→    pos_dm = np.asarray(ft_pos_dm, dtype=np.float32)\n   287→    bins = np.floor(pos_dm / 10.0).astype(np.int16)\n   288→    return np.clip(bins, 0, 3)\n   289→\n   290→\n   291→def binarize_licks(lick_frames: np.ndarray, trial_lick_mask: np.ndarray, frame_idx: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   292→    out = np.zeros(frame_idx.size, dtype=np.uint8)\n   293→    if frame_idx.size == 0:\n   294→        return out\n   295→\n   296→    frames = np.asarray(lick_frames[trial_lick_mask], dtype=np.int64)\n   297→    if frames.size == 0:\n   298→        return out\n   299→\n   300→    kept = np.intersect1d(frames, frame_idx, assume_unique=False)\n   301→    if kept.size == 0:\n   302→        return out\n   303→\n   304→    offsets = np.searchsorted(frame_idx, kept)\n   305→    valid = (offsets >= 0) & (offsets < frame_idx.size) & (frame_idx[offsets] == kept)\n   306→    if np.any(valid):\n   307→        out[offsets[valid]] = 1\n   308→    return out\n   309→\n   310→\n   311→def make_processing_plot(\n   312→    session_idx: int,\n   313→    session_id: str,\n   314→    beh: dict[str, Any],\n   315→    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   316→    input_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   317→    output_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   318→    speed_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   319→    speed_edges: np.ndarray,\n   320→) -> None:\n   321→    trial_count = len(neural_trials)\n   322→    if trial_count == 0:\n   323→        return\n   324→\n   325→    example_trial = min(5, trial_count - 1)\n   326→    example_neural = neural_trials[example_trial]\n   327→    example_input = input_trials[example_trial]\n   328→    example_output = output_trials[example_trial]\n   329→\n   330→    fig, axes = plt.subplots(3, 2, figsize=(16, 12))\n   331→\n   332→    nshow_trials = min(80, int(beh[\"ntrials\"]))\n   333→    cue_pos = np.asarray(beh[\"SoundPos\"][:nshow_trials], dtype=float) / 10.0\n   334→    rew_pos = np.asarray(beh[\"RewPos\"][:nshow_trials], dtype=float) / 10.0\n   335→    is_rew = np.asarray(beh[\"isRew\"][:nshow_trials], dtype=bool)\n   336→    axes[0, 0].scatter(cue_pos, np.arange(nshow_trials), s=6, c=\"purple\", label=\"cue\")\n   337→    axes[0, 0].scatter(rew_pos[is_rew], np.flatnonzero(is_rew), s=6, c=\"tab:blue\", label=\"reward\")\n   338→    axes[0, 0].set_xlim(0, 4)\n   339→    axes[0, 0].invert_yaxis()\n   340→    axes[0, 0].set_xlabel(\"Corridor position (m)\")\n   341→    axes[0, 0].set_ylabel(\"Trial\")\n   342→    axes[0, 0].set_title(\"Cue / reward positions\")\n   343→    axes[0, 0].legend(loc=\"upper right\")\n   344→\n   345→    nneurons_plot = min(64, example_neural.shape[0])\n   346→    vmax = np.percentile(example_neural[:nneurons_plot], 99)\n   347→    axes[0, 1].imshow(example_neural[:nneurons_plot], aspect=\"auto\", interpolation=\"nearest\", vmax=vmax)\n   348→    axes[0, 1].set_title(f\"Example neural trial {example_trial}\")\n   349→    axes[0, 1].set_xlabel(\"Frame\")\n   350→    axes[0, 1].set_ylabel(\"Neuron\")\n   351→\n   352→    axes[1, 0].plot(example_input[0], label=\"time_to_sound\")\n   353→    axes[1, 0].plot(example_input[2], label=\"time_since_start\")\n   354→    axes[1, 0].plot(example_input[3], label=\"reward_available\")\n   355→    axes[1, 0].set_title(\"Decoder inputs\")\n   356→    axes[1, 0].set_xlabel(\"Frame\")\n   357→    axes[1, 0].legend(loc=\"upper right\")\n   358→\n   359→    axes[1, 1].plot(example_output[1], label=\"licking\")\n   360→    axes[1, 1].plot(example_output[2], label=\"position_bin\")\n   361→    axes[1, 1].plot(example_output[3], label=\"speed_bin\")\n   362→    axes[1, 1].set_title(\"Decoder outputs\")\n   363→    axes[1, 1].set_xlabel(\"Frame\")\n   364→    axes[1, 1].legend(loc=\"upper right\")\n   365→\n   366→    all_speeds = np.concatenate(speed_trials) if speed_trials else np.array([])\n   367→    if all_speeds.size == 0:\n   368→        all_speeds = np.zeros(1)\n   369→    axes[2, 0].hist(all_speeds, bins=50, color=\"0.5\")\n   370→    for edge in speed_edges[1:-1]:\n   371→        axes[2, 0].axvline(edge, color=\"tab:red\", linestyle=\"--\", linewidth=1)\n   372→    axes[2, 0].set_title(\"Running speed distribution in session\")\n   373→    axes[2, 0].set_xlabel(\"Running speed\")\n   374→    axes[2, 0].set_ylabel(\"Count\")\n   375→\n   376→    pos_bins, pos_counts = np.unique(example_output[2], return_counts=True)\n   377→    axes[2, 1].bar(pos_bins, pos_counts, color=\"tab:green\")\n   378→    axes[2, 1].set_title(\"Example trial position bins\")\n   379→    axes[2, 1].set_xlabel(\"Position bin\")\n   380→    axes[2, 1].set_ylabel(\"Frames\")\n   381→\n   382→    fig.suptitle(f\"Processing diagnostics: session {session_idx} ({session_id})\")\n   383→    fig.tight_layout()\n   384→    fig.savefig(f\"processing_{session_id}.png\", dpi=150)\n   385→    plt.close(fig)\n   386→\n   387→\n   388→def process_session(spec: SessionSpec, session_idx: int) -> dict[str, Any]:\n   389→    t0 = time.time()\n   390→    planes = load_spike_planes(spec.raw_key)\n   391→    nframes = int(planes[0].shape[1])\n   392→    if any(int(arr.shape[1]) != nframes for arr in planes):\n   393→        raise ValueError(f\"Plane frame-count mismatch in session {spec.raw_key}\")\n   394→\n   395→    beh = spec.behavior_ref\n   396→    trial_frame_sets = get_trial_frame_indices(beh, nframes)\n   397→    iarea = load_retinotopy(spec.raw_key)\n   398→    total_neurons = int(sum(arr.shape[0] for arr in planes))\n   399→    if total_neurons != int(iarea.shape[0]):\n   400→        raise ValueError(\n   401→            f\"Neuron count mismatch in session {spec.raw_key}: planes sum to {total_neurons}, retinotopy has {iarea.shape[0]}\"\n   402→        )\n   403→\n   404→    sound_pos = np.asarray(beh[\"SoundPos\"][: int(beh[\"ntrials\"])], dtype=np.float32)\n   405→    wall_name = np.asarray(beh[\"WallName\"][: int(beh[\"ntrials\"])], dtype=object)\n   406→    is_rew = np.asarray(beh[\"isRew\"][: int(beh[\"ntrials\"])], dtype=np.int8)\n   407→    ft_pos = np.asarray(beh[\"ft_Pos\"][:nframes], dtype=np.float32)\n   408→    ft_speed = np.asarray(beh[\"ft_RunSpeed\"][:nframes], dtype=np.float32)\n   409→    lick_frames = np.asarray(beh[\"LickFr\"], dtype=int)\n   410→    lick_trials = np.asarray(beh[\"LickTrind\"], dtype=int)\n   411→\n   412→    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   413→    input_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   414→    output_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   415→    speed_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   416→\n   417→    for trial, frame_idx in enumerate(trial_frame_sets):\n   418→        if frame_idx.size == 0:\n   419→            continue\n   420→\n   421→        literal_wall = str(wall_name[trial])\n   422→        canonical_wall = spec.wall_to_stimulus[literal_wall]\n   423→        stimulus_idx = OUTPUT_STIM_TO_IDX[canonical_wall]\n   424→\n   425→        neural_trial = np.concatenate([plane[:, frame_idx] for plane in planes], axis=0).astype(np.float16, copy=False)\n   426→        trial_pos_dm = np.maximum(ft_pos[frame_idx], 0.0)\n   427→        t_sec = trial_pos_dm / CORRIDOR_SPEED_DM_PER_S\n   428→        cue_sec = (float(sound_pos[trial]) - trial_pos_dm) / CORRIDOR_SPEED_DM_PER_S\n   429→        reward_trial = np.full(frame_idx.size, float(is_rew[trial]), dtype=np.float16)\n   430→        day_trial = np.full(frame_idx.size, spec.training_day, dtype=np.float16)\n   431→        speed_trial = ft_speed[frame_idx].astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   432→\n   433→        input_trial = np.vstack(\n   434→            [\n   435→                cue_sec.astype(np.float16, copy=False),\n   436→                day_trial,\n   437→                t_sec.astype(np.float16, copy=False),\n   438→                reward_trial,\n   439→            ]\n   440→        )\n   441→\n   442→        lick_trial_mask = lick_trials == trial\n   443→        licking = binarize_licks(lick_frames, lick_trial_mask, frame_idx)\n   444→        pos_bins = position_to_bins(ft_pos[frame_idx]).astype(np.uint8, copy=False)\n   445→        output_trial = np.vstack(\n   446→            [\n   447→                np.full(frame_idx.size, stimulus_idx, dtype=np.uint8),\n   448→                licking,\n   449→                pos_bins,\n   450→                np.zeros(frame_idx.size, dtype=np.uint8),  # placeholder for speed bins\n   451→            ]\n   452→        )\n   453→\n   454→        neural_trials.append(neural_trial)\n   455→        input_trials.append(input_trial)\n   456→        output_trials.append(output_trial)\n   457→        speed_trials.append(speed_trial)\n   458→\n   459→    if len(neural_trials) < 2:\n   460→        raise ValueError(f\"Session {spec.raw_key} has fewer than 2 valid trials after processing\")\n   461→\n   462→    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n   463→    print(\n   464→        f\"[{session_idx:03d}] {spec.raw_key}: trials={len(neural_trials)} \"\n   465→        f\"neurons={total_neurons} frames/session={nframes} time={elapsed:.2f}s\",\n   466→        flush=True,\n   467→    )\n   468→\n   469→    return {\n   470→        \"session_id\": spec.raw_key,\n   471→        \"subject\": spec.subject,\n   472→        \"neural\": neural_trials,\n   473→        \"input\": input_trials,\n   474→        \"output\": output_trials,\n   475→        \"speed_trials\": speed_trials,\n   476→        \"brain_region_idx\": build_brain_region_idx(iarea),\n   477→        \"wall_to_stimulus\": dict(spec.wall_to_stimulus),\n   478→        \"reward_mode\": str(beh[\"Reward_Mode\"]),\n   479→        \"nframes\": nframes,\n   480→    }\n   481→\n   482→\n   483→def apply_speed_bins(processed_sessions: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> np.ndarray:\n   484→    all_speeds = np.concatenate(\n   485→        [speed for session in processed_sessions for speed in session[\"speed_trials\"]],\n   486→        axis=0,\n   487→    )\n   488→    quantiles = np.quantile(all_speeds, [0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0])\n   489→    quantiles[0] = min(quantiles[0], Any]:\n    t0 = time.time()\n    planes = load_spike_planes(spec.raw_key)\n    nframes = int(planes[0].shape[1])\n    if any(int(arr.shape[1]) != nframes for arr in planes):\n        raise        canonical_wall = spec.wall_to_stimulus[literal_wall]\n        stimulus_idx = OUTPUT_STIM_TO_IDX[canonical_wall]\n\n        neural_trial = np.concatenate([plane[:, frame_idx] for plane in planes], axis=0).astype(np.float16, copy=False)\n        trial_pos_dm = np.maximum(ft_pos[frame_idx], 0.0)\n        t_sec = trial_pos_dm / CORRIDOR_SPEED_DM_PER_S\n        cue_sec = `Figures.ipynb` plots figures.\n    49→- The code is for imaging mice (`Imaging_Exp_info.npy` is loaded in the notebook), so this is calcium-imaging-derived neural activity rather than electrophysiology.\n    50→- The published code does **not** recompute `dF/F`; it loads precomputed neural activity from session files via `load_spk`.\n    51→- `load_spk` concatenates arrays inside `np.load(...).item()['spks']`, so a session may be stored in multiple blocks/planes but is treated downstream as one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n    52→- The reference pipeline creates a spatially aligned representation:\n    53→  - Corridor length is 6 m.\n    54→  - Neural activity is interpolated into `60` bins (`1 decimeter` / `0.1 m` each).\n    55→  - Only frames with `beh['ft_move'] > 0` are used when building interpolated trial-by-position activity.\n    56→- Frame-level task masks used repeatedly in the reference:\n    57→  - `beh['ft_CorrSpc']`: texture corridor frames.\n    58→  - `beh['ft_GraySpc']`: gray-space frames.\n    59→  - `beh['ft_WallID']`: corridor identity / stimulus at each frame.\n    60→  - `beh['ft_trInd']`: trial index for each neural frame.\n    61→- Trial-splitting convention in the code:\n    62→  - “odd trials” in comments correspond to `ft_trInd % 2 == 0`.\n    63→  - The authors use one parity for training/selectivity estimation and the other parity for test/visualization.\n    64→- Relevant behavior fields documented in the notebook include corridor entry/exit frames (`StartFr`, `EndFr`), cue and reward frames (`SoundFr`, `SoundDelayFr`, `RewardFr`), per-frame running speed (`ft_RunSpeed`), per-frame position (`ft_Pos`, `ft_PosCum`), and lick frame/times (`LickFr`, `LickTime`, `LickPos`).\n    65→- No explicit neuron quality filtering is implemented in the public reference code beyond task- and area-based masks used for specific analyses.\n    66→- Retinotopy files provide neuron-wise area labels; sessions can include neurons from multiple areas.\n    67→\n    68→---\n    69→\n    70→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    71→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    72→\n    73→### Data Structure\n    74→- `/app/data/beh/`\n    75→  - `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`: experiment-to-session metadata mapping used by the reference notebook.\n    76→  - `Beh_<exp_type>.npy`: per-experiment behavior dictionaries. Each file maps session key -> session behavior structure.\n    77→  - `example_bef_and_aft_learning_behavior.npy`: example behavior-only file used in figure code.\n    78→  - `Unsupervised_pretraining_behavior/`: three behavior-only files (`Beh_no_pretrain.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_grat_image.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_nat_image.npy`) without matching imaging metadata in `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`.\n    79→- `/app/data/spk/`\n    80→  - One `*_neural_data.npy` file per raw imaging session.\n    81→  - Each file contains only `{'spks': [...]}` where `spks` is a list of arrays. In a representative file (`TX60_2021_04_10_1_neural_data.npy`) the list length is 3 and each entry has shape `(16244, 23469)`; the reference code concatenates along axis 0 to form one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n    82→- `/app/data/retinotopy/`\n    83→  - One `*_trans.npz` file per raw imaging session plus `areas.npz`.\n    84→  - Representative fields: `A`, `xpos`, `ypos`, `xy_t`, `iarea`.\n    85→  - `iarea` length matches neuron count from concatenated `spks` in the representative sample.\n    86→- `/app/data/process_data/`\n    87→  - Empty in the provided dataset; intermediate files used in the paper were not precomputed here.\n    88→\n    89→Behavior dictionary structure (representative session `TX60_2021_06_07_1` from `Beh_sup_test1.npy`):\n    90→- Scalar/session fields: `ntrials`, `Corridor_Length`, `Reward_Mode`, `Reward_Delay_ms`, `UniqWalls`, `stim_id`.\n    91→- Trial-level fields: `WallName`, `isRew`, `SoundFr`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, `RewardFr`, `SoundPos`, `SoundDelPos`, `RewPos`, `Trial_start_time`, `Trial_end_time`.\n    92→- Frame-level fields aligned to imaging frames: `ft`, `ft_trInd`, `ft_Pos`, `ft_PosCum`, `ft_RunSpeed`, `ft_move`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc`, `ft_WallID`, `BefCueFr`, `AftCueFr`.\n    93→- Event streams: `LickTime`, `LickFr`, `LickTrind`, `LickPos`.\n    94→\n    95→Session-key structure:\n    96→- Most behavior entries are keyed as `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`.\n    97→- Some entries include stimulus-type suffixes such as `_swap1` / `_swap2` via `stimtype`.\n    98→- The same raw imaging session can appear in multiple behavior files, and a minority of raw sessions have multiple distinct behavior variants.\n    99→\n   100→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n   101→| Statistic | Value |\n   102→|-----------|-------|\n   103→| mean 445.051; min 84; max 789 |\n   109→\n   110→Additional size notes:\n   111→- `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` contains 23 experiment groups.\n   112→- Across those 23 groups there are 142 Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   123→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   124→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   125→| Neurons (total) | 89 recordings total; 20,547-89,577 neurons per recording | “We performed 89 recordings in 19 mice...” / “from 20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording” |\n   126→| Neurons / session | 20,547-89,577 | “from 20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording” |\n   127→| Subjects | 19 mice | “We performed 89 recordings in 19 mice” |\n   128→| Sessions / subject | Not given as a single summary; varies by cohort | Paper reports 89 recordings total across 19 mice, and notes that some mice have multiple imaging sessions |\n   129→| Trials (total) | Not explicitly stated in paper | Not reported as a single dataset-wide number |\n   130→| Trials / session | Not explicitly stated in paper | Not reported as a single dataset-wide number |\n   131→| Neural data time bin | Not stated in paper/methods; reference notebook states calcium frame rate `fs = 3.17 Hz` (~315.46 ms/frame) | Notebook markdown: “Calcium signal recording frame rate: fs = 3.17Hz” |\n   132→| Behavior data time bin | No single fixed bin in text; events are continuous and aligned to imaging frames/positions | Behavior is described via event times, frame-aligned variables, and position interpolation |\n   133→| Reward rate | Task mice: reward available only in rewarded corridor after cue; unsupervised mice: no rewards | “water was available after the sound cue in rewarded trials only”; “did not receive water rewards” |\n   134→| Corridor geometry | 4 m textured corridor + 2 m grey space | “The virtual reality corridors were each 4 m long, with 2 m of grey space between corridors” |\n   135→| Sound cue position | Uniformly random from 0.5-3.5 m in imaging mice | “the time of the sound cue was randomly chosen per trial from a uniform distribution between positions 0.5 m and 3.5 m” |\n   136→| Running threshold / VR speed | 6 cm/s threshold; corridor moves at 60 cm/s while above threshold | “running faster than a threshold of 6 cm s−1” / “virtual corridors always moved at a constant speed (60 cm s−1)” |\n   137→| Selective-neuron threshold | d′ >= 0.3 (or <= -0.3) | “The criteria for selective neurons was d′ ≥ 0.3” |\n   138→| Reward-prediction cross-validation | 10-fold CV on leaf1 early- vs late-cue trials | “We randomly split all trials into tenfolds... used ninefolds as training trials...” |\n   139→\n   140→\n   141→### Processing Details\n   142→- Analyses are based on deconvolved fluorescence traces from Suite2p spike deconvolution, not raw fluorescence and not recomputed `dF/F`.\n   143→- For stimulus selectivity, the paper explicitly uses only the 0-4 m textured corridor and excludes non-running timepoints.\n   144→- The paper distinguishes between:\n   145→  - raw-frame analyses without interpolation for d′ selectivity;\n   146→  - position-interpolated trial-by-position matrices for sequence similarity, coding direction, and reward-prediction analyses.\n   147→- Spatial interpolation is described at 0.1 m resolution over 0-6 m for running-speed analyses; the notebook and code implement 60 bins over the full 6 m corridor.\n   148→- Cue position is the task-critical stochastic variable:\n   149→  - present on all trial types for imaging mice, including unsupervised cohorts;\n   150→  - used as a proxy for reward position in rewarded corridors because cue and reward positions are highly correlated.\n   151→- The paper’s reward-prediction analysis uses leaf1 trials split into early-cue versus late-cue trials and selects neurons with `d′late_vs_early >= 0.3`.\n   152→- Reward-prediction population responses are obtained with 10-fold cross-validation.\n   153→\n   154→### Curation Steps\n   155→\n   156→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   157→- No global public-code neuron QC beyond the upstream Suite2p pipeline is described.\n   158→- Selectivity-based analyses use thresholded `d′` values (for example `d′ >= 0.3`).\n   159→- Coding-direction analyses use the top 5% most selective neurons per class from train trials.\n   160→- Reward-prediction analyses additionally focus on anatomically defined anterior HVAs in the paper.\n   161→\n   162→**Trial curation rules**:\n   163→- Use only running timepoints for neural analyses.\n   164→- Stimulus-selectivity calculations use only the textured 0-4 m portion of the corridor.\n   165→- Sequence-similarity analyses split leaf1/circle1 trials into train/test halves, then odd/even trial partitions inside held-out test data.\n   166→- Reward-prediction analysis:\n   167→  - uses only leaf1 trials;\n   168→  - splits trials into early-cue vs late-cue groups based on cue position;\n   169→  - excludes one mouse for first-lick alignment because it had no trials with first lick later than 2 m;\n   170→  - excludes one mouse from leaf2 lick/no-lick comparison because it had only one leaf2 no-lick trial.\n   171→\n   172→### Decoders Trained\n   173→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   174→| No paper-reported classifier matching this task | N/A |\n   175→| Cross-validated reward-prediction population response | Qualitatively robust in task mice; no scalar accuracy reported |\n   176→| Coding-direction / similarity analyses | Projections and similarity indices reported; no scalar accuracy reported |\n   177→\n   178→Notes on expected decoder accuracy:\n   179→- The paper does not report a supervised decoder that directly predicts stimulus, licking, position, or speed from all neurons.\n   180→- Qualitative expectations from the paper are:\n   181→  - stimulus category should be decodable, especially after learning and in medial visual areas;\n   182→  - position within the corridor should be strongly represented as single-trial neural sequences;\n   183→  - licking and cue/reward-related signals should be present, especially in task cohorts;\n   184→  - reward-related temporal structure should be strongest in anterior HVAs.\n   185→\n   186→---\n   187→\n   188→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   189→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   190→\n   191→### Discrepancies Found\n   192→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   193→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   194→| Recording/session count | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` drives analyses by experiment grouping | 142 experiment-session entries, 99 unique behavior keys, 89 raw neural session files | “89 recordings in 19 mice” | Treat the raw imaging recording as the non-duplicated neural session unit. Do not duplicate sessions merely because the same recording is reused in several figure-specific behavior files. |\n   195→| Duplicate sessions across experiment files | Reference notebook iterates experiment groups independently | 33 raw recordings are reused across multiple `Beh_<exp_type>.npy` files; core arrays (`WallName`, `SoundPos`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, `isRew`) are identical across duplicates | Paper reuses the same recordings in multiple analyses/figures | Merge duplicates by raw session key when constructing the decoder dataset. Keep one canonical behavior instance per raw recording. |\n   196→| `_swap1` / `_swap2` sessions | Some behavior entries are separate keys with `stimtype` suffixes | In unsupervised/naive cohorts, `_swap1` and `_swap2` are alternate analysis views of the same trial arrays; only `stim_id` / `StimTrial` differs. In supervised test3, swap1 and swap2 can also appear as separate raw sessions on different dates. | Paper states swap stimuli in unsupervised/naive mice were in the same session but treated as separate data points for statistics; task mice introduced them separately | For decoder conversion, use the actual per-trial `WallName` labels from the canonical raw session and avoid duplicating identical neural recordings. Preserve both swap stimulus categories as distinct output labels when they truly occur in `WallName`. |\n   197→| Stimulus labels | Reference analyses often use `stim_id` and `StimTrial` to select a subset of categories relevant to a figure | Behavior files can contain more actual stimulus labels in `WallName` / `UniqWalls` than are assigned non-NaN `stim_id` values in a particular experiment view | Paper discusses different analyses focusing on specific stimulus subsets (for example leaf1 vs circle1, leaf1 vs leaf2, swaps) | Use `WallName` as the ground-truth per-trial visual stimulus category for decoder outputs. Use `stim_id` only when reproducing paper-specific selection logic. |\n   198→| Corridor length wording | Code uses 60 bins over full corridor and often restricts texture area to first 40 bins | Behavior files have `Corridor_Length = 60.0`; frame masks divide corridor into texture vs grey | Paper says corridors were 4 m long with 2 m grey space between corridors | These are consistent in decimeter units: 0-4 m texture + 4-6 m grey. For decoder outputs, full 0-6 m position is available and can be binned into 4 equal 1 m bins using the first 4 m of corridor progression from trial start if aligning to corridor entry. |\n   199→| Neural signal type | Code loads `spks`; no explicit fluorescence preprocessing in repo | `spk/*_neural_data.npy` stores deconvolved activity arrays | Paper says analyses use Suite2p deconvolved fluorescence traces | No `dF/F` recomputation is needed; use provided deconvolved activity matrices directly. |\n   200→| Running-time restriction | Code repeatedly masks with `ft_move > 0` and corridor masks | Behavior files provide `ft_move`, `ft_isMoving`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc` | Paper says only running timepoints were considered | Restrict framewise analyses to valid in-trial frame windows; preserve speed as an input/output variable, but note that neural interpolation in reference code uses moving frames only. |\n   201→| Reward structure | Behavior files contain both `Passive` and `Active after cue`, with delays 0/1000/1500 ms | `Reward_Mode`, `Reward_Delay_ms`, `isRew`, `RewPos`, `RewardFr` vary across sessions | Paper notes some mice received passive delayed rewards, others active rewards, but cue was present in all imaging trials | Use direct trial/frame variables from data (`isRew`, `SoundFr`, `RewardFr`, `Reward_Mode`) rather than assuming a single reward contingency. |\n   202→\n   203→Final understanding:\n   204→- The paper, code, and raw files agree on the core imaging cohort: 89 raw recordings from 19 mice.\n   205→- The public behavior files are organized for multiple figure-specific analyses, which creates duplicated “analysis views” of some raw sessions.\n   206→- The canonical decoder dataset should therefore be built around unique raw recordings with one behavior object per recording, while preserving all actual trial labels present in `WallName`.\n   207→- Reference processing to preserve:\n   208→  - use deconvolved traces as provided;\n   209→  - align trials to corridor entry (`StartFr`);\n   210→  - use the paper’s frame/position variables rather than reconstructing timing from scratch;\n   211→  - keep the distinction between textured corridor (0-4 m) and grey zone (4-6 m);\n   212→  - respect running-related masking where interpolation or framewise matching requires it.\n   213→\n   214→---\n   215→\n   216→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   217→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   218→\n   219→### Variable Mapping\n   220→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   221→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   222→| `spk_file['spks']` concatenated across planes | `neural` | Concatenate list elements along neuron axis; slice per trial using running corridor frame mask; store as `float16` | `load_spk` | Use provided deconvolved traces directly; no `dF/F` recomputation; decoder converts back to `float32` internally |\n   223→| `beh['ft_trInd'] == trial`, `beh['ft_CorrSpc']`, and `beh['ft_move'] > 0` | Trial time axis for `neural`, `input`, `output` | Select running frames inside the corridor that belong to the trial; this defines aligned timepoints | Paper Methods + `fr_valid = VRmove & isCorridor` in `utils.py` | Chosen instead of `StartFr:EndFr` because `StartFr` points one frame before first in-trial frame and because the reference analyses exclude non-running periods |\n   224→| `beh['ft']` and `beh['SoundFr']` | `input[0]` = `time_to_sound_cue_s` | `ft[SoundFr] - ft[selected_frames]`, converted from days to seconds | Sound cue fields in methods + behavior schema | Continuous, time-varying; negative after cue |\n   225→| Session date / block within mouse | `input[1]` = `training_day` | Continuous day index per session = days since mouse’s first imaging date + small block offset if needed | Derived from `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Per-trial scalar, replicated by decoder internally if needed |\n   226→| `beh['ft']` | `input[2]` = `time_since_trial_start_s` | `ft[selected_frames] - ft[first_selected_frame]`, in seconds | Trial alignment to corridor entry | Continuous, time-varying |\n   227→| `beh['isRew'][trial]` | `input[3]` = `reward_available` | Cast to {0,1} | Behavior fields / paper reward structure | Per-trial corridor identity, not actual reward delivery |\n   228→| Merged per-session `WallName` -> canonical label map from all behavior views | `output[0]` = `visual_stimulus` | Map literal wall names to canonical category IDs | `stim_id`, `StimTrial`, paper experiment structure | Session-specific mapping avoids cross-file duplication and handles rock/wood remapping |\n   229→| `beh['LickFr']`, `beh['LickTrind']` | `output[1]` = `licking` | Binary vector over selected frames; 1 if >=1 lick on that frame | Lick fields in behavior schema | Time-varying |\n   230→| `beh['ft_Pos']` | `output[2]` = `position_bin` | Discretize corridor position into 4 bins: [0,1), [1,2), [2,3), [3,4] m | Paper corridor geometry | Running corridor frames ensure 4 equal 1 m bins over textured region while matching reference frame selection |\n   231→| `beh['ft_RunSpeed']` | `output[3]` = `speed_bin` | Global quartile binning across included frames | Running speed fields + paper running analyses | Time-varying; thresholds computed from included dataset frames |\n   232→| `retinotopy['iarea']` | `brain_region_idx` | Map codes to `V1`, `mHV`, `lHV`, `aHV`, `other` | `neu_area_ID` | Preserve neurons with codes `-1` or `7` as `other` rather than dropping them |\n   233→| Mouse name from session metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Unique subject list + per-session index | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | One subject per raw session |\n   234→\n   235→### Key Decisions\n   236→1. **Session unit = unique raw imaging recording (89 total)**: The paper and raw files agree on 89 recordings; repeated behavior entries across experiment files are reused analysis views of the same recording and should not become duplicate decoder sessions.\n   237→2. **Canonical behavior for a raw session will be built by merging all behavior views of that raw session**: Core arrays are identical across duplicates, but `stim_id`/`StimTrial` expose different subsets of categories. Merging recovers a complete per-session label map without duplicating neural data.\n   238→3. **Visual stimulus output will use session-specific canonical categories, not literal wall names alone**: This preserves cross-mouse comparability when some mice use `rock/wood` stimuli and when some sessions reverse literal circle/leaf naming. The category space will be `circle1`, `circle2`, `circle3`, `leaf1`, `leaf2`, `leaf3`, `leaf1_swap1`, `leaf1_swap2`.\n   239→4. **`circle3` will be preserved as its own category**: It appears in actual trial labels but is not assigned a canonical `stim_id` in the provided figure-specific views; treating it as a separate presented stimulus preserves information rather than forcing an unsupported remapping.\n   240→5. **Trial frames will be selected with `(ft_trInd == trial) & ft_CorrSpc & (ft_move > 0)`**: This matches the paper and reference code, which restrict imaging analyses to running frames inside the textured corridor, while still aligning the trial to corridor entry rather than to `StartFr`.\n   241→6. **The decoder dataset will use running frames within the corridor (0-4 m texture region)**: This follows the published imaging analyses and removes reward-stop periods that the Methods say were excluded from analysis.\n   242→7. **Neural activity will remain on the original frame timebase after running-frame selection**: The task is temporally aligned to corridor entry and requires time-varying cue, licking, and speed outputs; framewise traces are therefore more appropriate than position-interpolated spike maps for the decoder, while still using the same valid-frame mask as the reference analyses.\n   243→8. **All neurons will be retained**: The public reference code does not apply additional global neuron QC beyond Suite2p preprocessing; region labels are metadata, not a filter.\n   244→9. **Brain-region mapping will follow the reference coarse groups with an additional `other` bucket**: The published helper only uses four named groups for some figures, but the raw data contains many neurons with codes `-1` and `7`; these should be retained and labeled explicitly.\n   245→10. **Running speed bins will be computed globally over included frames**: Global quartiles give consistent output classes across sessions, which is better suited to a cross-session decoder than per-session bins.\n   246→11. **Time variables will use actual frame timestamps (`ft`) rather than nominal frame count alone**: This preserves small timestamp jitter and makes cue-aligned timing exact.\n   247→12. **Day-of-training input will be mouse-relative continuous session day**: Using days since first imaging session (with a tiny block offset if needed) respects within-mouse chronology and preserves the intended “training day” context.\n   248→\n   249→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   250→- [ ] Verify for duplicated raw sessions across behavior files that `WallName`, `SoundPos`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, and `isRew` are identical (`np.allclose` / `np.array_equal`).\n   251→- [ ] Verify that merged per-session stimulus mappings cover every unique `WallName` actually present in that session.\n   252→- [ ] Verify that selected frames satisfy `ft_CorrSpc == True`, `ft_move > 0`, and `ft_trInd == trial`, and that `StartFr` is typically one frame earlier than the first corridor-assigned frame in the reference behavior.\n   253→- [ ] Verify that binary lick outputs match raw `LickFr` / `LickTrind` on spot-checked trials using `np.allclose`.\n   254→- [ ] Verify that time-to-cue reaches 0 at the frame corresponding to `SoundFr` on spot-checked trials.\n   255→- [ ] Verify that per-neuron brain-region indices match retinotopy array lengths and reference area grouping rules.\n   256→\n   257→---\n   258→\n   259→## Step 6: Script Development\n   260→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   261→\n   262→Implementation notes:\n   263→- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` with required CLI:\n   264→  - `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n   265→  - `--sample`\n   266→  - `--full` (default behavior when `--sample` is absent)\n   267→  - `--show-processing`\n   268→- Script structure:\n   269→  - builds unique raw-session specs from `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` and all `Beh_*.npy` files;\n   270→  - verifies duplicated behavior views are identical on core arrays;\n   271→  - merges per-session stimulus mappings across behavior views;\n   272→  - processes one raw session at a time;\n   273→  - slices trial data with `ft_trInd == trial`, `ft_CorrSpc`, and `ft_move > 0`;\n   274→  - derives cue-relative and trial-relative time inputs from corridor position at the reference 60 cm/s virtual speed rather than from wall-clock timestamps, so excluded pause periods do not inflate decoder times;\n   275→  - saves decoder-format pickle with metadata.\n   276→- The script was compile-checked with `python3 -m py_compile /app/convert_data.py`.\n   277→- After correcting the frame-selection mask to match the reference running analyses and changing stored dtypes (`neural`/`input` to `float16`, compact integer metadata/outputs), the sample run succeeded:\n   278→  - `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n   279→  - output: 2 sessions, 893 trials, 110,041 neurons, total run time about 0.45 min.\n   280→\n   281→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   282→- Full-session spike concatenation would have created an extra multi-GB copy per session.\n   283→- Computing speed quartiles before trial conversion would have required an extra pass with ambiguous frame truncation relative to neural data length.\n   284→- Storing dense per-trial arrays as `float32` made the full pickle exceed 100 GB and pushed Step 9 well beyond the allowed runtime estimate.\n   285→\n   286→Code speedups added:\n   287→- Process spike planes directly and concatenate only per-trial slices, avoiding a full-session concatenated copy.\n   288→- Process one session at a time to keep peak intermediate memory bounded.\n   289→- Defer speed binning until after session conversion, using only the already collected per-trial speed arrays.\n   290→- Add session-level parallel processing for `--full` mode (up to 4 workers) because sessions are independent and the serial full-run estimate exceeded 15 minutes.\n   291→- Store neural and input trial arrays as `float16` because `train_decoder.py` materializes its own `float32` buffers during training, so the lower on-disk precision cuts serialization cost without changing the decoder path.\n   292→\n   293→---\n   294→\n   295→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   296→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   297→\n   298→### Sample and produced degenerate reward/lick outputs; this was fixed by changing `--sample` session selection to choose representative task sessions with reward variation and in-corridor licking.\n   324→- After re-checking `utils.py`, the converter was updated to keep only running corridor frames (`ft_move > 0`) rather than all corridor frames. This reduced the sample pickle from about 6.8 GB to about 2.33 GB and matches the published imaging analysis mask more closely.\n   325→- A later review found that using raw `ft` timestamps after removing non-running frames preserved long idle pauses in the time inputs. This was corrected by converting corridor position to time using the paper’s fixed virtual speed of 60 cm/s, bringing sample input ranges to the expected order of seconds.\n   326→\n   327→### Run Time Estimates\n   328→\n   329→| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n   330→| | |\n   331→| Session-level full-mode parallelism (`max_workers = 4`) | Reduced estimated full conversion from about 18.0 min serial to about 4.5 min by file-size scaling |\n   332→| Running-frame mask + `float16` storage | Reduced sample pickle from about 6.8 GB to about 2.33 GB and should cut full-dataset serialization and load time by roughly 3x |\n   333→| Representative lighter sample sessions for validation | Reduced sample conversion from about 1.0 min to about 0.45 min and made sample decoder training practical |\n   334→\n   335→| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n   336→| | | |\n   337→| Sample conversion (`TX60_2021_06_07_1`, `TX61_2021_06_07_2`) | 8.61 s, 11.71 s | 0.45 min observed for 2 sessions |\n   338→| Full conversion (serial estimate by raw spike-file size ratio) | ~12.1 s/session equivalent by sample scaling | ~17.95 min |\n   339→| Full conversion (4-worker estimate by same scaling, before mask/dtype fix) | parallel | ~4.49 min compute plus oversized serialization |\n   340→| Full conversion (4-worker estimate after mask/dtype fix) | parallel | expected to be materially lower in serialization cost; |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `load_exp_beh` | `/app/code/utils.py` | LOADING | Loads per-experiment behavior dictionary from `beh/Beh_<exp_type>.npy`. |\n| `load_spk` | `/app/code/utils.py` | LOADING | Loads session neural activity from `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>_neural_data.npy` and concatenates `['spks']` across planes into one neuron-by-frame matrix. |\n| `load_retino` | `/app/code/utils.py` | LOADING | Loads retinotopy transform and maps neurons to coarse visual areas using `iarea`. |\n| `get_interpPos_spk` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | Interpolates neural activity from frame time to trial-position bins; outputs `(neurons, trials, 60)` using cumulative position and only moving frames. |\n| `spk_pos_interp` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | Core interpolation helper used by `get_interpPos_spk`; linearly resamples each neuron along cumulative position normalized by corridor length. |\n| `Get_dprime_selective_neuron` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | Uses raw frame activity in corridor frames during movement to compare stimulus conditions; defines stimulus selectivity. |\n| `Get_coding_direction` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | 6 m corridor from behavior and neural data. |\n\n### Notes\n- `/app/code/README.md` states `data_process_script.ipynb` shows how intermediate results are processed and saved; `Figures.ipynb` plots figures.\n- The code is for imaging mice (`Imaging_Exp_info.npy` is loaded in the notebook), so this is calcium-imaging-derived neural activity rather than electrophysiology.\n- The published code does **not** recompute `dF/F`; it loads precomputed neural activity from session files via `load_spk`.\n- `load_spk` concatenates arrays inside `np.load(...).item()['spks']`, so a session may be stored in multiple blocks/planes but is treated downstream as one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n- The reference pipeline creates a spatially aligned representation:\n  - Corridor length is 6 m.\n  - Neural activity is interpolated into `60` bins (`1 decimeter` / `0.1 m` each).\n  - Only frames with `beh['ft_move'] > 0` are used when building interpolated trial-by-position activity.\n- Frame-level task masks used repeatedly in the reference:\n  - `beh['ft_CorrSpc']`: texture corridor frames.\n  - `beh['ft_GraySpc']`: gray-space frames.\n  - `beh['ft_WallID']`: corridor identity / stimulus at each frame.\n  - `beh['ft_trInd']`: trial index for each neural frame.\n- Trial-splitting convention in the code:\n  - “odd trials” in comments correspond to `ft_trInd % 2 == 0`.\n  - The authors use one parity for training/selectivity estimation and the other parity for test/visualization.\n- Relevant behavior fields documented in the notebook include corridor entry/exit frames (`StartFr`, `EndFr`), cue and reward frames (`SoundFr`, `SoundDelayFr`, `RewardFr`), per-frame running speed (`ft_RunSpeed`), per-frame position (`ft_Pos`, `ft_PosCum`), and lick frame/times (`LickFr`, `LickTime`, `LickPos`).\n- No explicit neuron quality filtering is implemented in the public reference code beyond task- and area-based masks used for specific analyses.\n- Retinotopy files provide neuron-wise area labels; sessions can include neurons from multiple areas.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/beh/`\n  - `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`: experiment-to-session metadata mapping used by the reference notebook.\n  - `Beh_<exp_type>.npy`: per-experiment behavior dictionaries. Each file maps session key -> session behavior structure.\n  - `example_bef_and_aft_learning_behavior.npy`: example behavior-only file used in figure code.\n  - `Unsupervised_pretraining_behavior/`: three behavior-only files (`Beh_no_pretrain.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_grat_image.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_nat_image.npy`) without matching imaging metadata in `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`.\n- `/app/data/spk/`\n  - One `*_neural_data.npy` file per raw imaging session.\n  - Each file contains only `{'spks': [...]}` where `spks` is a list of arrays. In a representative file (`TX60_2021_04_10_1_neural_data.npy`) the list length is 3 and each entry has shape `(16244, 23469)`; the reference code concatenates along axis 0 to form one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n- `/app/data/retinotopy/`\n  - One `*_trans.npz` file per raw imaging session plus `areas.npz`.\n  - Representative fields: `A`, `xpos`, `ypos`, `xy_t`, `iarea`.\n  - `iarea` length matches neuron count from concatenated `spks` in the representative sample.\n- `/app/data/process_data/`\n  - Empty in the provided dataset; intermediate files used in the paper were not precomputed here.\n\nBehavior dictionary structure (representative session `TX60_2021_06_07_1` from `Beh_sup_test1.npy`):\n- Scalar/session fields: `ntrials`, `Corridor_Length`, `Reward_Mode`, `Reward_Delay_ms`, `UniqWalls`, `stim_id`.\n- Trial-level fields: `WallName`, `isRew`, `SoundFr`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, `RewardFr`, `SoundPos`, `SoundDelPos`, `RewPos`, `Trial_start_time`, `Trial_end_time`.\n- Frame-level fields aligned to imaging frames: `ft`, `ft_trInd`, `ft_Pos`, `ft_PosCum`, `ft_RunSpeed`, `ft_move`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc`, `ft_WallID`, `BefCueFr`, `AftCueFr`.\n- Event streams: `LickTime`, `LickFr`, `LickTrind`, `LickPos`.\n\nSession-key structure:\n- Most behavior entries are keyed as `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`.\n- Some entries include stimulus-type suffixes such as `_swap1` / `_swap2` via `stimtype`.\n- The same raw imaging session can appear in multiple behavior files, and a minority of raw sessions have multiple distinct behavior variants.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 4,691,034 across 89 raw imaging sessions |\n| Neurons / session | mean 52,708.247; min 20,547; max 89,577 |\n| Subjects | 19 unique mice |\n| Sessions / subject | raw sessions: mean 4.684 (range 1-8); behavior-session variants: mean 5.211 (range 1-12) |\n| Trials (total) | 44,060 across 99 unique behavior-session keys |\n| Trials / session | mean 445.051; min 84; max 789 |\n\nAdditional size notes:\n- `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` contains 23 experiment groups.\n- Across those 23 groups there are 142 experiment-session entries, but only 99 unique behavior-session keys and 89 unique raw imaging session files.\n- 9 raw imaging sessions have multiple behavior variants (for example `_swap1` and `_swap2`).\n- 33 raw imaging session files are reused across more than one experiment grouping.\n- Representative per-subject raw session counts: `TX119` 8 raw / 10 behavior variants, `TX123` 8 raw / 12 behavior variants, `DR10` 6 raw / 7 behavior variants.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 89 recordings total; 20,547-89,577 neurons per recording | “We performed 89 recordings in 19 mice...” / “from 20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording” |\n| Neurons / session | 20,547-89,577 | “from 20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording” |\n| Subjects | 19 mice | “We performed 89 recordings in 19 mice” |\n| Sessions / subject | Not given as a single summary; varies by cohort | Paper reports 89 recordings total across 19 mice, and notes that some mice have multiple imaging sessions |\n| Trials (total) | Not explicitly stated in paper | Not reported as a single dataset-wide number |\n| Trials / session | Not explicitly stated in paper | Not reported as a single dataset-wide number |\n| Neural data time bin | Not stated in paper/methods; reference notebook states calcium frame rate `fs = 3.17 Hz` (~315.46 ms/frame) | Notebook markdown: “Calcium signal recording frame rate: fs = 3.17Hz” |\n| Behavior data time bin | No single fixed bin in text; events are continuous and aligned to imaging frames/positions | Behavior is described via event times, frame-aligned variables, and position interpolation |\n| Reward rate | Task mice: reward available only in rewarded corridor after cue; unsupervised mice: no rewards | “water was available after the sound cue in rewarded trials only”; “did not receive water rewards” |\n| Corridor geometry | 4 m textured corridor + 2 m grey space | “The virtual reality corridors were each 4 m long, with 2 m of grey space between corridors” |\n| Sound cue position | Uniformly random from 0.5-3.5 m in imaging mice | “the time of the sound cue was randomly chosen per trial from a uniform distribution between positions 0.5 m and 3.5 m” |\n| Running threshold / VR speed | 6 cm/s threshold; corridor moves at 60 cm/s while above threshold | “running faster than a threshold of 6 cm s−1” / “virtual corridors always moved at a constant speed (60 cm s−1)” |\n| Selective-neuron threshold | d′ >= 0.3 (or <= -0.3) | “The criteria for selective neurons was d′ ≥ 0.3” |\n| Reward-prediction cross-validation | 10-fold CV on leaf1 early- vs late-cue trials | “We randomly split all trials into tenfolds... used ninefolds as training trials...” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Analyses are based on deconvolved fluorescence traces from Suite2p spike deconvolution, not raw fluorescence and not recomputed `dF/F`.\n- For stimulus selectivity, the paper explicitly uses only the 0-4 m textured corridor and excludes non-running timepoints.\n- The paper distinguishes between:\n  - raw-frame analyses without interpolation for d′ selectivity;\n  - position-interpolated trial-by-position matrices for sequence similarity, coding direction, and reward-prediction analyses.\n- Spatial interpolation is described at 0.1 m resolution over 0-6 m for running-speed analyses; the notebook and code implement 60 bins over the full 6 m corridor.\n- Cue position is the task-critical stochastic variable:\n  - present on all trial types for imaging mice, including unsupervised cohorts;\n  - used as a proxy for reward position in rewarded corridors because cue and reward positions are highly correlated.\n- The paper’s reward-prediction analysis uses leaf1 trials split into early-cue versus late-cue trials and selects neurons with `d′late_vs_early >= 0.3`.\n- Reward-prediction population responses are obtained with 10-fold cross-validation.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- No global public-code neuron QC beyond the upstream Suite2p pipeline is described.\n- Selectivity-based analyses use thresholded `d′` values (for example `d′ >= 0.3`).\n- Coding-direction analyses use the top 5% most selective neurons per class from train trials.\n- Reward-prediction analyses additionally focus on anatomically defined anterior HVAs in the paper.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Use only running timepoints for neural analyses.\n- Stimulus-selectivity calculations use only the textured 0-4 m portion of the corridor.\n- Sequence-similarity analyses split leaf1/circle1 trials into train/test halves, then odd/even trial partitions inside held-out test data.\n- Reward-prediction analysis:\n  - uses only leaf1 trials;\n  - splits trials into early-cue vs late-cue groups based on cue position;\n  - excludes one mouse for first-lick alignment because it had no trials with first lick later than 2 m;\n  - excludes one mouse from leaf2 lick/no-lick comparison because it had only one leaf2 no-lick trial.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| No paper-reported classifier matching this task | N/A |\n| Cross-validated reward-prediction population response | Qualitatively robust in task mice; no scalar accuracy reported |\n| Coding-direction / similarity analyses | Projections and similarity indices reported; no scalar accuracy reported |\n\nNotes on expected decoder accuracy:\n- The paper does not report a supervised decoder that directly predicts stimulus, licking, position, or speed from all neurons.\n- Qualitative expectations from the paper are:\n  - stimulus category should be decodable, especially after learning and in medial visual areas;\n  - position within the corridor should be strongly represented as single-trial neural sequences;\n  - licking and cue/reward-related signals should be present, especially in task cohorts;\n  - reward-related temporal structure should be strongest in anterior HVAs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Recording/session count | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` drives analyses by experiment grouping | 142 experiment-session entries, 99 unique behavior keys, 89 raw neural session files | “89 recordings in 19 mice” | Treat the raw imaging recording as the non-duplicated neural session unit. Do not duplicate sessions merely because the same recording is reused in several figure-specific behavior files. |\n| Duplicate sessions across experiment files | Reference notebook iterates experiment groups independently | 33 raw recordings are reused across multiple `Beh_<exp_type>.npy` files; core arrays (`WallName`, `SoundPos`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, `isRew`) are identical across duplicates | Paper reuses the same recordings in multiple analyses/figures | Merge duplicates by raw session key when constructing the decoder dataset. Keep one canonical behavior instance per raw recording. |\n| `_swap1` / `_swap2` sessions | Some behavior entries are separate keys with `stimtype` suffixes | In unsupervised/naive cohorts, `_swap1` and `_swap2` are alternate analysis views of the same trial arrays; only `stim_id` / `StimTrial` differs. In supervised test3, swap1 and swap2 can also appear as separate raw sessions on different dates. | Paper states swap stimuli in unsupervised/naive mice were in the same session but treated as separate data points for statistics; task mice introduced them separately | For decoder conversion, use the actual per-trial `WallName` labels from the canonical raw session and avoid duplicating identical neural recordings. Preserve both swap stimulus categories as distinct output labels when they truly occur in `WallName`. |\n| Stimulus labels | Reference analyses often use `stim_id` and `StimTrial` to select a subset of categories relevant to a figure | Behavior files can contain more actual stimulus labels in `WallName` / `UniqWalls` than are assigned non-NaN `stim_id` values in a particular experiment view | Paper discusses different analyses focusing on specific stimulus subsets (for example leaf1 vs circle1, leaf1 vs leaf2, swaps) | Use `WallName` as the ground-truth per-trial visual stimulus category for decoder outputs. Use `stim_id` only when reproducing paper-specific selection logic. |\n| Corridor length wording | Code uses 60 bins over full corridor and often restricts texture area to first 40 bins | Behavior files have `Corridor_Length = 60.0`; frame masks divide corridor into texture vs grey | Paper says corridors were 4 m long with 2 m grey space between corridors | These are consistent in decimeter units: 0-4 m texture + 4-6 m grey. For decoder outputs, full 0-6 m position is available and can be binned into 4 equal 1 m bins using the first 4 m of corridor progression from trial start if aligning to corridor entry. |\n| Neural signal type | Code loads `spks`; no explicit fluorescence preprocessing in repo | `spk/*_neural_data.npy` stores deconvolved activity arrays | Paper says analyses use Suite2p deconvolved fluorescence traces | No `dF/F` recomputation is needed; use provided deconvolved activity matrices directly. |\n| Running-time restriction | Code repeatedly masks with `ft_move > 0` and corridor masks | Behavior files provide `ft_move`, `ft_isMoving`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc` | Paper says only running timepoints were considered | Restrict framewise analyses to valid in-trial frame windows; preserve speed as an input/output variable, but note that neural interpolation in reference code uses moving frames only. |\n| Reward structure | Behavior files contain both `Passive` and `Active after cue`, with delays 0/1000/1500 ms | `Reward_Mode`, `Reward_Delay_ms`, `isRew`, `RewPos`, `RewardFr` vary across sessions | Paper notes some mice received passive delayed rewards, others active rewards, but cue was present in all imaging trials | Use direct trial/frame variables from data (`isRew`, `SoundFr`, `RewardFr`, `Reward_Mode`) rather than assuming a single reward contingency. |\n\nFinal understanding:\n- The paper, code, and raw files agree on the core imaging cohort: 89 raw recordings from 19 mice.\n- The public behavior files are organized for multiple figure-specific analyses, which creates duplicated “analysis views” of some raw sessions.\n- The canonical decoder dataset should therefore be built around unique raw recordings with one behavior object per recording, while preserving all actual trial labels present in `WallName`.\n- Reference processing to preserve:\n  - use deconvolved traces as provided;\n  - align trials to corridor entry (`StartFr`);\n  - use the paper’s frame/position variables rather than reconstructing timing from scratch;\n  - keep the distinction between textured corridor (0-4 m) and grey zone (4-6 m);\n  - respect running-related masking where interpolation or framewise matching requires it.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spk_file['spks']` concatenated across planes | `neural` | Concatenate list elements along neuron axis; slice per trial using running corridor frame mask; store as `float16` | `load_spk` | Use provided deconvolved traces directly; no `dF/F` recomputation; decoder converts back to `float32` internally |\n| `beh['ft_trInd'] == trial`, `beh['ft_CorrSpc']`, and `beh['ft_move'] > 0` | Trial time axis for `neural`, `input`, `output` | Select running frames inside the corridor that belong to the trial; this defines aligned timepoints | Paper Methods + `fr_valid = VRmove & isCorridor` in `utils.py` | Chosen instead of `StartFr:EndFr` because `StartFr` points one frame before first in-trial frame and because the reference analyses exclude non-running periods |\n| `beh['ft']` and `beh['SoundFr']` | `input[0]` = `time_to_sound_cue_s` | `ft[SoundFr] - ft[selected_frames]`, converted from days to seconds | Sound cue fields in methods + behavior schema | Continuous, time-varying; negative after cue |\n| Session date / block within mouse | `input[1]` = `training_day` | Continuous day index per session = days since mouse’s first imaging date + small block offset if needed | Derived from `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Per-trial scalar, replicated by decoder internally if needed |\n| `beh['ft']` | `input[2]` = `time_since_trial_start_s` | `ft[selected_frames] - ft[first_selected_frame]`, in seconds | Trial alignment to corridor entry | Continuous, time-varying |\n| `beh['isRew'][trial]` | `input[3]` = `reward_available` | Cast to {0,1} | Behavior fields / paper reward structure | Per-trial corridor identity, not actual reward delivery |\n| Merged per-session `WallName` -> canonical label map from all behavior views | `output[0]` = `visual_stimulus` | Map literal wall names to canonical category IDs | `stim_id`, `StimTrial`, paper experiment structure | Session-specific mapping avoids cross-file duplication and handles rock/wood remapping |\n| `beh['LickFr']`, `beh['LickTrind']` | `output[1]` = `licking` | Binary vector over selected frames; 1 if >=1 lick on that frame | Lick fields in behavior schema | Time-varying |\n| `beh['ft_Pos']` | `output[2]` = `position_bin` | Discretize corridor position into 4 bins: [0,1), [1,2), [2,3), [3,4] m | Paper corridor geometry | Running corridor frames ensure 4 equal 1 m bins over textured region while matching reference frame selection |\n| `beh['ft_RunSpeed']` | `output[3]` = `speed_bin` | Global quartile binning across included frames | Running speed fields + paper running analyses | Time-varying; thresholds computed from included dataset frames |\n| `retinotopy['iarea']` | `brain_region_idx` | Map codes to `V1`, `mHV`, `lHV`, `aHV`, `other` | `neu_area_ID` | Preserve neurons with codes `-1` or `7` as `other` rather than dropping them |\n| Mouse name from session metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Unique subject list + per-session index | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | One subject per raw session |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session unit = unique raw imaging recording (89 total)**: The paper and raw files agree on 89 recordings; repeated behavior entries across experiment files are reused analysis views of the same recording and should not become duplicate decoder sessions.\n2. **Canonical behavior for a raw session will be built by merging all behavior views of that raw session**: Core arrays are identical across duplicates, but `stim_id`/`StimTrial` expose different subsets of categories. Merging recovers a complete per-session label map without duplicating neural data.\n3. **Visual stimulus output will use session-specific canonical categories, not literal wall names alone**: This preserves cross-mouse comparability when some mice use `rock/wood` stimuli and when some sessions reverse literal circle/leaf naming. The category space will be `circle1`, `circle2`, `circle3`, `leaf1`, `leaf2`, `leaf3`, `leaf1_swap1`, `leaf1_swap2`.\n4. **`circle3` will be preserved as its own category**: It appears in actual trial labels but is not assigned a canonical `stim_id` in the provided figure-specific views; treating it as a separate presented stimulus preserves information rather than forcing an unsupported remapping.\n5. **Trial frames will be selected with `(ft_trInd == trial) & ft_CorrSpc & (ft_move > 0)`**: This matches the paper and reference code, which restrict imaging analyses to running frames inside the textured corridor, while still aligning the trial to corridor entry rather than to `StartFr`.\n6. **The decoder dataset will use running frames within the corridor (0-4 m texture region)**: This follows the published imaging analyses and removes reward-stop periods that the Methods say were excluded from analysis.\n7. **Neural activity will remain on the original frame timebase after running-frame selection**: The task is temporally aligned to corridor entry and requires time-varying cue, licking, and speed outputs; framewise traces are therefore more appropriate than position-interpolated spike maps for the decoder, while still using the same valid-frame mask as the reference analyses.\n8. **All neurons will be retained**: The public reference code does not apply additional global neuron QC beyond Suite2p preprocessing; region labels are metadata, not a filter.\n9. **Brain-region mapping will follow the reference coarse groups with an additional `other` bucket**: The published helper only uses four named groups for some figures, but the raw data contains many neurons with codes `-1` and `7`; these should be retained and labeled explicitly.\n10. **Running speed bins will be computed globally over included frames**: Global quartiles give consistent output classes across sessions, which is better suited to a cross-session decoder than per-session bins.\n11. **Time variables will use actual frame timestamps (`ft`) rather than nominal frame count alone**: This preserves small timestamp jitter and makes cue-aligned timing exact.\n12. **Day-of-training input will be mouse-relative continuous session day**: Using days since first imaging session (with a tiny block offset if needed) respects within-mouse chronology and preserves the intended “training day” context.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Verify for duplicated raw sessions across behavior files that `WallName`, `SoundPos`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, and `isRew` are identical (`np.allclose` / `np.array_equal`).\n- [ ] Verify that merged per-session stimulus mappings cover every unique `WallName` actually present in that session.\n- [ ] Verify that selected frames satisfy `ft_CorrSpc == True`, `ft_move > 0`, and `ft_trInd == trial`, and that `StartFr` is typically one frame earlier than the first corridor-assigned frame in the reference behavior.\n- [ ] Verify that binary lick outputs match raw `LickFr` / `LickTrind` on spot-checked trials using `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Verify that time-to-cue reaches 0 at the frame corresponding to `SoundFr` on spot-checked trials.\n- [ ] Verify that per-neuron brain-region indices match retinotopy array lengths and reference area grouping rules.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nImplementation notes:\n- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` with required CLI:\n  - `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n  - `--sample`\n  - `--full` (default behavior when `--sample` is absent)\n  - `--show-processing`\n- Script structure:\n  - builds unique raw-session specs from `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` and all `Beh_*.npy` files;\n  - verifies duplicated behavior views are identical on core arrays;\n  - merges per-session stimulus mappings across behavior views;\n  - processes one raw session at a time;\n  - slices trial data with `ft_trInd == trial`, `ft_CorrSpc`, and `ft_move > 0`;\n  - derives cue-relative and trial-relative time inputs from corridor position at the reference 60 cm/s virtual speed rather than from wall-clock timestamps, so excluded pause periods do not inflate decoder times;\n  - saves decoder-format pickle with metadata.\n- The script was compile-checked with `python3 -m py_compile /app/convert_data.py`.\n- After correcting the frame-selection mask to match the reference running analyses and changing stored dtypes (`neural`/`input` to `float16`, compact integer metadata/outputs), the sample run succeeded:\n  - `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n  - output: 2 sessions, 893 trials, 110,041 neurons, total run time about 0.45 min.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Full-session spike concatenation would have created an extra multi-GB copy per session.\n- Computing speed quartiles before trial conversion would have required an extra pass with ambiguous frame truncation relative to neural data length.\n- Storing dense per-trial arrays as `float32` made the full pickle exceed 100 GB and pushed Step 9 well beyond the allowed runtime estimate.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Process spike planes directly and concatenate only per-trial slices, avoiding a full-session concatenated copy.\n- Process one session at a time to keep peak intermediate memory bounded.\n- Defer speed binning until after session conversion, using only the already collected per-trial speed arrays.\n- Add session-level parallel processing for `--full` mode (up to 4 workers) because sessions are independent and the serial full-run estimate exceeded 15 minutes.\n- Store neural and input trial arrays as `float16` because `train_decoder.py` materializes its own `float32` buffers during training, so the lower on-disk precision cuts serialization cost without changing the decoder path.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n###
