the times are MATLAB datenums, so in days\nFRAME_RATE = 3.17                # imaging rate, from the reference notebook\n\n# Some animals sit still for a really long time in the middle of a trial\n# Median trial length is 23 frames, but some sit for 5000 frames.\n# Remove trials that are outliers in trial length\nTRIAL_LENGTH_PERCENTILE = 99\n\n# the wall of a corridor is one of four textures, shown as several crops and spatial shuffles\nTEXTURE = {'circle1': 'circle', 'circle2': 'circle', 'circle3': 'circle',\n           'leaf1': 'leaf', 'leaf2': 'leaf', 'leaf3': 'leaf',\n           'leaf1_swap1': 'leaf', 'leaf1_swap2': 'leaf',\n           'rock1': 'rock', 'rock2': 'rock',\n           'wood1': 'wood', 'wood2': 'wood', 'wood5': 'wood',\n           'wood1_swap1': 'wood', 'wood1_swap2': 'wood'}\n\nSTIMULUS = ['circle', 'leaf', 'rock', 'wood']\nLICKING = ['no lick', 'lick']\nPOSITION = ['0-1 m', '1-2 m', '2-3 m', '3-4 m']\nSPEED = ['lowest', 'low', 'high', 'highest']\n\n# the coarse visual areas of the reference, as the retinotopy codes them\nAREAS = {'V1': [8], 'mHV': [0, 1, 2, 9], 'lHV': [5, 6], 'aHV': [3, 4]}\nBRAIN_REGIONS = list(AREAS)\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_to_sound_cue', 'day_of_training', 'time_since_trial_start', 'reward_availability']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['visual_stimulus', 'licking', 'position', 'running_speed']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [STIMULUS, LICKING, POSITION, SPEED]\n\n\n# --- sessions ---\n\ndef list_sessions(datadir):\n    \"\"\"Every recording once, as (subject, session_id, beh_file, beh_key).\n\n    datadir: directory holding the beh/, spk/ and retinotopy/ subfolders.\n    \"\"\"\n    # the master index of every recording, grouped by experiment type\n    exp_info = np.load(os.path.join(datadir, 'beh', 'Imaging_Exp_info.npy'),\n                       allow_pickle=True).item()\n\n    records, seen = [], set()\n    for exp_type, db in exp_info.items():\n        for entry in db:\n            # a recording is one mouse on one date in one block, and names the spike file\n            session_id = '%s_%s_%s' % (entry['mname'], entry['datexp'], entry['blk'])\n\n            # the same recording is listed under several experiment types, so keep only the first\n            if session_id in seen:\n                continue\n            seen.add(session_id)\n\n            # the behavior file keys a swap session by its stimulus type as well\n            beh_key = session_id + ('_' + entry['stimtype'] if 'stimtype' in entry else '')\n            records.append((entry['mname'], session_id, 'Beh_%s.npy' % exp_type, beh_key))\n\n    return sorted(records)\n\n\ndef training_days(records):\n    \"\"\"How many days into training each session is, counted per mouse.\"\"\"\n    days, count = {}, {}\n\n    # a session id sorts by date within a mouse, so the sessions are counted in order\n    for subject, session_id, _, _ in sorted(records):\n        days[session_id] = count.get(subject, 0)\n        count[subject] = days[session_id] + 1\n\n    return days\n\n\n# --- trials ---\n\ndef trial_frames(beh, trial, n_frames):\n    \"\"\"Neural frames of one trial, from corridor entry to the grey space.\"\"\"\n    # the authors label every frame with its trial and whether it is inside the texture\n    inside = (beh['ft_trInd'][:n_frames] == trial) & beh['ft_CorrSpc'][:n_frames]\n    return np.flatnonzero(inside)\n\n\ndef get_all_trial_lengths(records, datadir):\n    \"\"\"Compute the length of every trial in the dataset, in imaging frames.\n\n    Collect all trial lengths based on behavior data (approximately matches neural data)\n\n    records: list of (subject, session_id, beh_file, beh_key), every session of the dataset.\n    datadir: directory holding the beh/, spk/ and retinotopy/ subfolders.\n\n    Returns an int array of per trial frame counts, without the trials that were never imaged.\n    \"\"\"\n    # a behavior file holds several sessions, so it is read once for all of them\n    groups = {}\n    for record in records:\n        groups.setdefault(record[2], []).append(record)\n\n    lengths = []\n    for beh_file, group in groups.items():\n        behavior = np.load(os.path.join(datadir, 'beh', beh_file), allow_pickle=True).item()\n        for _, _, _, beh_key in group:\n            beh = behavior[beh_key]\n            n_frames = beh['ft_trInd'].size          # the behavior grid, so no spike file needed\n            for trial in range(beh['ntrials']):\n                size = trial_frames(beh, trial, n_frames).size\n                if size:\n                    lengths.append(size)\n        del behavior\n\n    return np.array(lengths)\n\n\ndef get_max_trial_length(records, datadir):\n    \"\"\"Compute longest trial to keep, as a percentile over every trial in the dataset.\n\n    Measured over the whole dataset even when only a sample is converted, so that a sample and a\n    full run agree on which trials to keep.\n\n    records: list of (subject, session_id, beh_file, beh_key), every session of the dataset.\n    datadir: directory holding the beh/, spk/ and retinotopy/ subfolders.\n\n    Returns the limit in frames as a float, and the lengths it was measured from.\n    \"\"\"\n    lengths = get_all_trial_lengths(records, datadir)\n    return np.percentile(lengths, TRIAL_LENGTH_PERCENTILE), lengths\n\n\ndef quartiles(values):\n    \"\"\"Split into four bins of a quarter of the data each.\"\"\"\n    # a quarter or more of the frames can sit at exactly zero speed, and no threshold can\n    # cut a tie like that into equal parts, so the split is on rank rather than on value\n    rank = np.empty(values.size, dtype=int)\n    rank[np.argsort(values, kind='stable')] = np.arange(values.size)\n    return rank * 4 // values.size\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_spikes(session_id, datadir):\n    \"\"\"Deconvolved traces of the neurons of one session, and the visual area of each.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the beh/, spk/ and retinotopy/ subfolders.\n    \"\"\"\n    # the traces come one imaging plane at a time, in the order the retinotopy indexes them\n    planes = np.load(os.path.join(datadir, 'spk', '%s_neural_data.npy' % session_id),\n                     allow_pickle=True).item()\n    spikes = np.concatenate(planes['spks'], 0)\n\n    mouse, year, month, day, _ = session_id.split('_')\n    area = np.load(os.path.join(datadir, 'retinotopy',\n                                '%s_%s_%s_%s_trans.npz' % (mouse, year, month, day)))['iarea']\n\n    # a neuron outside the four areas, or with no area at all, is dropped\n    region = np.full(area.size, -1)\n    for index, name in enumerate(BRAIN_REGIONS):\n        region[np.isin(area, AREAS[name])] = index\n\n    keep = region >= 0\n    return spikes[keep], region[keep]\n\n\n# --- session ---\n\ndef process_session(subject, session_id, beh, day, datadir, max_trial_length):\n    \"\"\"One session as lists of per trial neural, input and output arrays.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the beh/, spk/ and retinotopy/ subfolders.\n    max_trial_length: longest trial to keep, in frames, from get_max_trial_length.\n    \"\"\"\n    spikes, region = load_spikes(session_id, datadir)\n\n    # the behavior can run past the imaging, so every stream is cut to the frames that were imaged\n    n_frames = spikes.shape[1]\n    frame_time = (beh['ft'][:n_frames] - beh['ft'][0]) * SEC_PER_DAY\n\n    # a trial whose corridor was not imaged has no frames left, and one longer than the limit is\n    # an animal that stopped rather than one that ran slowly, so neither is a traversal\n    frames = [trial_frames(beh, trial, n_frames) for trial in range(beh['ntrials'])]\n    trials = [trial for trial in range(beh['ntrials'])\n              if 0 < frames[trial].size <= max_trial_length]\n    if not trials:\n        return {'session_id': session_id, 'subject': subject, 'day': day,\n                'neural': [], 'input': [], 'output': [],\n                'n_trials': 0, 'n_units': region.size, 'regions': region}\n    kept = np.concatenate([frames[trial] for trial in trials])\n\n    # licking comes as the frame number of each lick, so make it a flag per frame\n    licking = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=int)\n    lick_frame = beh['LickFr'].astype(int)\n    licking[lick_frame[lick_frame < n_frames]] = 1\n\n    # position bins are 10 decimeters, the 1 m the task asks for\n    position = np.clip(beh['ft_Pos'][:n_frames] // 10, 0, 3).astype(int)\n\n    # the quartiles are over the frames the dataset keeps, so each bin holds a quarter of it\n    speed = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=int)\n    speed[kept] = quartiles(beh['ft_RunSpeed'][kept])\n\n    stimulus = np.array([STIMULUS.index(TEXTURE[name]) for name in beh['WallName']])\n    reward = beh['isRew'].astype(int)\n\n    # both events fall between frames, so they are interpolated onto the time axis\n    index = np.arange(n_frames)\n    start = np.interp(beh['StartFr'], index, frame_time)\n    cue = np.interp(beh['SoundFr'], index, frame_time)\n\n    neural, inputs, outputs = [], [], []\n    for trial in trials:\n        window = frames[trial]\n        n_frames = window.size\n        time = frame_time[window]\n\n        neural.append(spikes[:, window].astype(np.float16))\n        inputs.append(np.stack([cue[trial] - time,\n                                np.full(n_frames, day),\n                                time - start[trial],\n                                np.full(n_frames, reward[trial])]).astype(np.float32))\n        outputs.append(np.stack([np.full(n_frames, stimulus[trial]),\n                                 licking[window],\n                                 position[window],\n                                 speed[window]]).astype(np.int8))\n\n    return {'session_id': session_id, 'subject': subject, 'day': day,\n            'neural': neural, 'input': inputs, 'output': outputs,\n            'n_trials': len(neural), 'n_units': region.size, 'regions': region}\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

As neural decoders generally make use of temporally contiguous data, we do not drop frames in the middle of trials. \n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. A trial past the 99th percentile is 95% stationary, against 4% for a typical trial, so the distribution separates rather than shading off. The extreme case, trial 391 of `TX88_2022_07_19_1`, runs 5607 frames: the mouse enters, reaches 1.4 m of the 4 m corridor, stands there for 29 minutes and then runs out, contributing 5571 bins that all carry the same position label. Dropping such a trial whole is preferred to shortening it, which would keep a few hundred bins of a parked animal and invent an endpoint the traversal never had. The threshold is measured from the data rather than written down, over every session even when only a sample is converted, so that a sample run and a full one agree about which trials are too long; it comes out at 238.9 frames, 75 s, about ten times the median traversal, and removes 382 trials of 38110. The decoder reads each trial's length off its own array and treats every bin as an independent sample, so a shared window buys nothing. list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. defines stimulus selectivity. |\n| `Get_coding_direction` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | Uses odd/even trial splits, corridor-only moving frames, and interpolated spike maps for stimulus decoding analyses. |\n| `get_kfold_reward_response` | `/app/code/utils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds reward-related decoding responses from interpolated spike maps and cue/reward aligned trial variables. |\n| `neu_area_ID` | `/app/code/utils.py` | CURATION | Collapses retinotopy area IDs into `V1`, `mHV`, `lHV`, `aHV`. |\n| Notebook cell “get interpolational neural activity” | `/app/code/data_process_script.ipynb` | PROCESSING | Batch-generates saved interpolated spike files with 60 bins across 6 m corridor from behavior and neural data. |\n\n### Notes\n- `/app/code/README.md` states `data_process_script.ipynb` shows how intermediate results are processed and saved; `Figures.ipynb` plots figures.\n- The code is for imaging mice (`Imaging_Exp_info.npy` is loaded in the notebook), so this is calcium-imaging-derived neural activity rather than electrophysiology.\n- The published code does **not** recompute `dF/F`; it loads precomputed neural activity from session files via `load_spk`.\n- `load_spk` concatenates arrays inside `np.load(...).item()['spks']`, so a session may be stored in multiple blocks/planes but is treated downstream as one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n- The reference pipeline creates a spatially aligned representation:\n  - Corridor length is 6 m.\n  - Neural activity is interpolated into `60` bins (`1 decimeter` / `0.1 m` each).\n  - Only frames with `beh['ft_move'] > 0` are used when building interpolated trial-by-position activity.\n- Frame-level task masks used repeatedly in the reference:\n  - `beh['ft_CorrSpc']`: texture corridor frames.\n  - `beh['ft_GraySpc']`: gray-space frames.\n  - `beh['ft_WallID']`: corridor identity / stimulus at each frame.\n  - `beh['ft_trInd']`: trial index for each neural frame.\n- Trial-splitting convention in the code:\n  - “odd trials” in comments correspond to `ft_trInd % 2 == 0`.\n  - The authors use one parity for training/selectivity estimation and the other parity for test/visualization.\n- Relevant behavior fields documented in the notebook include corridor entry/exit frames (`StartFr`, `EndFr`), cue and reward frames (`SoundFr`, `SoundDelayFr`, `RewardFr`), per-frame running speed (`ft_RunSpeed`), per-frame position (`ft_Pos`, `ft_PosCum`), and lick frame/times (`LickFr`, `LickTime`, `LickPos`).\n- No explicit neuron quality filtering is implemented in the public reference code beyond task- and area-based masks used for specific analyses.\n- Retinotopy files provide neuron-wise area labels; sessions can include neurons from multiple areas.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/beh/`\n  - `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`: experiment-to-session metadata mapping used by the reference notebook.\n  - `Beh_<exp_type>.npy`: per-experiment behavior dictionaries. Each file maps session key -> session behavior structure.\n  - `example_bef_and_aft_learning_behavior.npy`: example behavior-only file used in figure code.\n  - `Unsupervised_pretraining_behavior/`: three behavior-only files (`Beh_no_pretrain.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_grat_image.npy`, `Beh_pretrain_on_nat_image.npy`) without matching imaging metadata in `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`.\n- `/app/data/spk/`\n  - One `*_neural_data.npy` file per raw imaging session.\n  - Each file contains only `{'spks': [...]}` where `spks` is a list of arrays. In a representative file (`TX60_2021_04_10_1_neural_data.npy`) the list length is 3 and each entry has shape `(16244, 23469)`; the reference code concatenates along axis 0 to form one neuron-by-frame matrix.\n- `/app/data/retinotopy/`\n  - One `*_trans.npz` file per raw imaging session plus `areas.npz`.\n  - Representative fields: `A`, `xpos`, `ypos`, `xy_t`, `iarea`.\n  - `iarea` length matches neuron count from concatenated `spks` in the representative sample.\n- `/app/data/process_data/`\n  - Empty in the provided dataset; min 84; max 789 |\n\nAdditional size notes:\n- `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` contains 23 experiment groups.\n- Across those 23 groups there are 142 experiment-session entries, but only 99 unique behavior-session keys and 89 unique raw imaging session files.\n- 9 raw imaging sessions have multiple behavior variants (for example `_swap1` and `_swap2`).\n- 33 raw imaging session files are reused across more than one experiment grouping.\n- Representative per-subject raw session counts: `TX119` 8 raw / 10 behavior variants, `TX123` 8 raw / 12 behavior variants, `DR10` 6 raw / 7 behavior variants.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 89 recordings total; the notebook and code implement 60 bins over the full 6 m corridor.\n- Cue position is the task-critical stochastic variable:\n  - present on all trial types for imaging mice, including unsupervised cohorts;\n  - used as a proxy for reward position in rewarded corridors because cue and reward positions are highly correlated.\n- The paper’s reward-prediction analysis uses leaf1 trials split into early-cue versus late-cue trials and selects neurons with `d′late_vs_early >= 0.3`.\n- Reward-prediction population responses are obtained with 10-fold cross-validation.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- No global public-code neuron QC beyond the upstream Suite2p pipeline is described.\n- Selectivity-based analyses use thresholded `d′` values (for example `d′ >= 0.3`).\n- Coding-direction analyses use the top 5% most selective neurons per class from train trials.\n- Reward-prediction analyses additionally focus on anatomically defined anterior HVAs in the paper.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Use only running timepoints for neural analyses.\n- Stimulus-selectivity calculations use only the textured 0-4 m portion of the corridor.\n- Sequence-similarity analyses split leaf1/circle1 trials into train/test halves, then odd/even trial partitions inside held-out test data.\n- Reward-prediction analysis:\n  - uses only leaf1 trials;\n  - splits trials into early-cue vs late-cue groups based on cue position;\n  - excludes one mouse for first-lick alignment because it had no trials with first lick later than 2 m;\n  - excludes one mouse from leaf2 lick/no-lick comparison because it had only one leaf2 no-lick trial.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| No paper-reported classifier matching this task | N/A |\n| Cross-validated reward-prediction population response | Qualitatively robust in task mice; repeated behavior entries across experiment files are reused analysis views of the same recording and should not become duplicate decoder sessions.\n2. **Canonical behavior for a raw session will be built by merging all behavior views of that raw session**: Core arrays are identical across duplicates, but `stim_id`/`StimTrial` expose different subsets of categories. Merging recovers a complete per-session label map without duplicating neural data.\n3. **Visual stimulus output will use session-specific canonical categories, not literal wall names alone**: This preserves cross-mouse comparability when some mice use `rock/wood` stimuli and when some sessions reverse literal circle/leaf naming. The category space will be `circle1`, `circle2`, `circle3`, `leaf1`, `leaf2`, `leaf3`, `leaf1_swap1`, `leaf1_swap2`.\n4. **`circle3` will be preserved as its own category**: It appears in actual trial labels but is not assigned a canonical `stim_id` in the provided figure-specific views; treating it as a separate presented stimulus preserves information rather than forcing an unsupported remapping.\n5. **Trial frames will be selected with `(ft_trInd == trial) & ft_CorrSpc & (ft_move > 0)`**: This matches the paper and reference code, which restrict imaging analyses to running frames inside the textured corridor, while still aligning the trial to corridor entry rather than to `StartFr`.\n6. **The decoder dataset will use running frames within the corridor (0-4 m texture region)**: This follows the published imaging analyses and removes reward-stop periods that the Methods say were excluded from analysis.\n7. **Neural activity will remain on the original frame timebase after running-frame selection**: The task is temporally aligned to corridor entry and requires time-varying cue, licking, and speed outputs; framewise traces are therefore more appropriate than position-interpolated spike maps for the decoder, while still using the same valid-frame mask as the reference analyses.\n8. **All neurons will be retained**: The public reference code does not apply additional global neuron QC beyond Suite2p preprocessing; region labels are metadata, not a filter.\n9. **Brain-region mapping will follow the reference coarse groups with an additional `other` bucket**: The published helper only uses four named groups for some figures, but the raw data contains many neurons with codes `-1` and `7`; these should be retained and labeled explicitly.\n10. **Running speed bins will be computed globally over included frames**: Global quartiles give consistent output classes across sessions, which is better suited to a cross-session decoder than per-session bins.\n11. **Time variables will use actual frame timestamps (`ft`) rather than nominal frame count alone**: This preserves small timestamp jitter and makes cue-aligned timing exact.\n12. **Day-of-training input will be mouse-relative continuous session day**: Using days since first imaging session (with a tiny block offset if needed) respects within-mouse chronology and preserves the intended “training day” context.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Verify for duplicated raw sessions across behavior files that `WallName`, `SoundPos`, `StartFr`, `EndFr`, and `isRew` are identical (`np.allclose` / `np.array_equal`).\n- [ ] Verify that merged per-session stimulus mappings cover every unique `WallName` actually present in that session.\n- [ ] Verify that selected frames satisfy `ft_CorrSpc == True`, `ft_move > 0`, and `ft_trInd == trial`, and that `StartFr` is typically one frame earlier than the first corridor-assigned frame in the reference behavior.\n- [ ] Verify that binary lick outputs match raw `LickFr` / `LickTrind` on spot-checked trials using `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Verify that time-to-cue reaches 0 at the frame corresponding to `SoundFr` on spot-checked trials.\n- [ ] Verify that per-neuron brain-region indices match retinotopy array lengths and reference area grouping rules.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

 plt.close(fig)\n\n\ndef process_session(spec: SessionSpec, session_idx: int) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    t0 = time.time()\n    planes = load_spike_planes(spec.raw_key)\n    nframes = int(planes[0].shape[1])\n    if any(int(arr.shape[1]) != nframes for arr in planes):\n        raise ValueError(f\"Plane frame-count mismatch in session {spec.raw_key}\")\n\n    beh = dtype=np.float32)\n    lick_frames = np.asarray(beh[\"LickFr\"], dtype=int)\n    lick_trials = np.asarray(beh[\"LickTrind\"], dtype=int)\n\n    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    input_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    output_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    speed_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\n\n    for trial, frame_idx in enumerate(trial_frame_sets):\n        if frame_idx.size == 0:\n            continue\n\n        literal_wall = str(wall_name[trial])\n        canonical_wall = spec.wall_to_stimulus[literal_wall]\n        stimulus_idx = OUTPUT_STIM_TO_IDX[canonical_wall]\n\n        neural_trial = np.concatenate([plane[:, frame_idx] for plane in planes], axis=0).astype(np.float16, copy=False)\n        trial_pos_dm = np.maximum(ft_pos[frame_idx], 0.0)\n        t_sec = trial_pos_dm / CORRIDOR_SPEED_DM_PER_S\n        cue_sec = (float(sound_pos[trial]) - trial_pos_dm) / CORRIDOR_SPEED_DM_PER_S\n        reward_trial = np.full(frame_idx.size, float(is_rew[trial]), dtype=np.float16)\n        day_trial = np.full(frame_idx.size, spec.training_day, dtype=np.float16)\n        speed_trial = ft_speed[frame_idx].astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n\n        input_trial = np.vstack(\n            [\n                cue_sec.astype(np.float16, copy=False),\n                day_trial,\n                t_sec.astype(np.float16, copy=False),\n                reward_trial,\n            ]\n        )\n\n        lick_trial_mask = lick_trials == trial\n        licking = binarize_licks(lick_frames, lick_trial_mask, frame_idx)\n        pos_bins =  }\n\n\ndef apply_speed_bins(processed_sessions: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> np.ndarray:\n    all_speeds = np.concatenate(\n        [speed for session in processed_sessions for speed in session[\"speed_trials\"]],\n        axis=0,\n    )\n    quantiles = np.quantile(all_speeds, [0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0])\n    quantiles[0] = min(quantiles[0], np.min(all_speeds))\n    quantiles[-1] = max(quantiles[-1], np.max(all_speeds))\n\n    for session in processed_sessions:\n        for trial_idx, speed_trial in enumerate(session[\"speed_trials\"]):\n            bins = binning until after session conversion, using only the already collected per-trial speed arrays.\n- Add session-level parallel processing for `--full` mode (up to 4 workers) because sessions are How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n     4\t\n     5\ti. Everything is read from three directories under `data`: `beh` for the behavior, `spk` for the deconvolved calcium traces and `retinotopy` for the visual area of each neuron. `beh/Imaging_Exp_info.npy` is the master index and is read first, listing every recording grouped by experiment type. Each `Beh_<exp_type>.npy` holds the behavior of several sessions keyed by that id, so the conversion loops over behavior files and reads each one once; the spikes and the retinotopy are read per session.\n     6\t\n     7\tii. How are the data split into subjects?\n    23\t\n    24\ti. The mouse name is taken from `mname` in the index entry and carried on every record, and the records are grouped by it. The index already names the mouse, so no split has to be derived.\n    36\t\n    37\t## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n    38\t\n    39\ti. A session is one mouse on one date in one block, which the index entry gives as `mname`, `datexp` and `blk`. The id built from the three names the spike file and keys the behavior. A recording listed under more than one experiment type is kept only the first time it is seen, leaving 89 sessions.\n    40\t\n    41\tii. The id, and the check that keeps each recording once:\n    42\t```python\n    43\tsession_id = '%s_%s_%s' % (entry['mname'], entry['datexp'], entry['blk'])\n    44\tif session_id in seen:\n    45\t    continue\n    46\tseen.add(session_id)\n    47\t```\n    48\t\n    49\tiii. The recording sometimes repeat under different behavior session type. Therefore a recording that is listed under more than one experiment type is kept only the first time it is seen.\n    50\t\n    51\t## 1-d. Are the data correctly split into trials?\n    52\t\n    53\ti. The split into trials is given by the data: every neural frame carries its trial in `ft_trInd`, and the boundaries are listed as frame numbers as well, `StartFr` at corridor entry, `GrayFr` at the grey space and `EndFr` at the exit. Based on the instruction we define the trials and the sequence between entry to the end of texture (4m). The trials are taken as they are, and within each one the frames kept are every frame inside the texture, `ft_CorrSpc`. Nothing is cut from a trial: it runs to the end of its own traversal, so trials differ in length.\n    54\t\n    55\tii. The trials are the ones the data declares, `ntrials` of them, indexed by `ft_trInd`:\n    56\t```python\n    57\tframes = [trial_frames(beh, trial, n_frames) for trial in range(beh['ntrials'])]\n    58\t```\n    59\t\n    60\tand the frames of each are the ones it labels with that trial:\n    61\t```python\n    62\tinside = (beh['ft_trInd'][:n_frames] == trial) & beh['ft_CorrSpc'][:n_frames]\n    63\treturn np.flatnonzero(inside)\n    64\t```\n    65\t\n    66\tiii. Trial goes from start to either gray or end frame, as described in the paper. As neural decoders generally make use of temporally contiguous data, we do not drop frames in the middle of trials. \n    67\t\n    68\t## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n    69\t\n    70\ti. Two trials are dropped: one left with no frames once the behavior is cut to the frames that were imaged, and one longer than the 99th percentile of traversals across the whole dataset. Nothing else is filtered.\n    71\t\n    72\tii. The two conditions, and the limit they use:\n    73\t```python\n    74\ttrials = [trial for trial in range(beh['ntrials'])\n    75\t          if 0 < frames[trial].size <= limit]\n    76\t```\n    77\t```python\n    78\tlengths = get_all_trial_lengths(records, datadir)\n    79\treturn np.percentile(lengths, TRIAL_LENGTH_PERCENTILE), lengths\n    80\t```\n    81\t\n    82\tiii. The longest traversals are not slow runs, they are animals that stopped. A trial past the 99th percentile is 95% stationary, against 4% for a typical trial, so the distribution separates rather than shading off. The extreme case, trial 391 of `TX88_2022_07_19_1`, runs 5607 frames: the mouse enters, reaches 1.4 m of the 4 m corridor, stands there for 29 minutes and then runs out, contributing 5571 bins that all carry the same position label. Dropping such a trial whole is preferred to shortening it, which would keep a few hundred bins of a parked animal and invent an endpoint the traversal never had. The threshold is measured from the data rather than written down, over every session even when only a sample is converted, so that a sample run and a full one agree about which trials are too long; it comes out at 238.9 frames, 75 s, about ten times the median traversal, and removes 382 trials of 38110. No session loses more than 9.5% of its trials and none falls under the two needed to evaluate a decoder.\n    83\t\n    84\t## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the final `neural` data derived from?\n    85\t\n    86\ti. From `spks` in `spk/<session_id>_neural_data.npy`, which is a list of one neurons by frames array per imaging plane, concatenated into a single array. The visual area of each neuron comes from `iarea` in `retinotopy/<mouse>_<date>_trans.npz`.\n    87\t\n    88\tii. The two reads and the concatenation:\n    89\t```python\n    90\tplanes = np.load(os.path.join(SPK, '%s_neural_data.npy' % session_id), allow_pickle=True).item()\n    91\tspikes = np.concatenate(planes['spks'], 0)\n    92\tarea = np.load(os.path.join(RETINO, '%s_%s_%s_%s_trans.npz' % (mouse, year, month, day)))['iarea']\n    93\t```\n    94\t\n    95\tiii. The neural data is read directly, nothing is derived from it.\n    96\t\n    97\t## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n    98\t\n    99\ti. The traces are not processed. The columns belonging to a trial are taken and the result is stored as float16. The trials are variable length and are left that way, so nothing is padded.\n   100\t\n   101\tii. One trial of neural data:\n   102\t```python\n   103\tneural.append(spikes[:, window].astype(np.float16))\n   104\t```\n   105\t\n   106\tiii. The file already holds deconvolved traces, which the methods state every analysis was based on, so no dF/F or deconvolution is needed. float16 rather than float32 because the dataset is quite big. \n   107\t\n   108\t## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n   109\t\n   110\ti. The only filter is the visual area. A neuron is kept if its `iarea` falls in V1, mHV, lHV or aHV, and dropped otherwise, which keeps 4,105,393 of 4,691,034 neurons.\n   111\t\n   112\tii. The area of each neuron, and the neurons kept:\n   113\t```python\n   114\tregion = np.full(area.size, -1)\n   115\tfor index, name in enumerate(BRAIN_REGIONS):\n   116\t    region[np.isin(area, AREAS[name])] = index\n   117\t\n   118\tkeep = region >= 0\n   119\treturn spikes[keep], region[keep]\n   120\t```\n   121\t\n   122\tiii. The authors already curated the neurons with the Suite2p cell classifier, and the reference code applies no further filter, so nothing is done beyond assigning the area.\n   123\t\n   124\t## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n   125\t\n   126\ti. The alignment event is corridor entry. Trials are of variable length, because mice run at different speeds, and they are stored at that length: every trial starts at its own corridor entry and ends where its traversal ends. Nothing is cut and nothing is padded.\n   127\t\n   128\tii. Every frame of the traversal, from corridor entry, and the trial built from exactly those frames:\n   129\t```python\n   130\tinside = (beh['ft_trInd'][:n_frames] == trial) & beh['ft_CorrSpc'][:n_frames]\n   131\treturn np.flatnonzero(inside)\n   132\t```\n   133\t```python\n   134\tneural.append(spikes[:, window].astype(np.float16))\n   135\t```\n   136\t\n   137\tiii. The format does not need one length for every trial. It asks that the bin *size* be the same across trials and sessions, which it is at 315 ms, and it accepts `None` for `off_end` when the trials share no common end. The decoder reads each trial's length off its own array and treats every bin as an independent sample, so a shared window buys nothing. A fixed window costs a great deal: at 32 frames it discarded 435407 real frames, 31.7% of everything inside a corridor, and replaced them with 281757 padding bins, 23.1% of what was written. Trials keep their own length instead, and the small number that are too long to be a traversal at all are dropped whole, as described in 1-e.\n   138\t\n   139\t## 2-e. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n   140\t\n   141\ti. It is not binned or resampled. The imaging frames are the bins: one column of `spks` per frame at 3.17 Hz, so a bin is 315 ms, and a trial holds as many of them as its traversal lasted, a median of 23.\n   142\t\n   143\tii. The rate, and the bin size it gives the metadata:\n   144\t```python\n   145\tFRAME_RATE = 3.17                # imaging rate, from the reference\n   146\t```\n   147\t```python\n   148\t'time_bin_size': 1000.0 / FRAME_RATE,        # ms\n   149\t```\n   150\t\n   151\tiii. The imaging frame is the finest resolution the data has, and every behavior stream is already on the same grid, so there is nothing to resample.\n   152\t\n   153\t## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *time_to_sound_cue* derived from?\n   154\t\n   155\ti. From `SoundFr`, the frame at which the sound cue was played in each trial, and `ft`, the timestamp of every imaging frame.\n   156\t\n   157\tii. The cue frame is interpolated onto the frame time axis, and the input is the cue time minus the time of each bin, in seconds.  Note that here the instruction as *time_to_sound_cue*, so it's positive before the sound cue, and negative after the sound cue.\n   168\t\n   169\tii. The time axis of the trial, and the input built from it:\n   170\t```python\n   171\tframe_time = (beh['ft'][:n_frames] - beh['ft'][0]) * SEC_PER_DAY\n   172\tcue = np.interp(beh['SoundFr'], index, frame_time)\n   173\t```\n   174\t\n   175\t```python\n   176\ttime = frame_time[window]\n   177\t```\n   178\t```python\n   179\tcue[trial] - time\n   180\t```\n   181\t\n   182\tiii. The same frames as the neural data of the trial:\n   189\t```python\n   190\ttime = frame_time[window]\n   191\t```\n   192\t\n   193\tiii. The date string is the only field that orders every session of every mouse, so it is used to put them in order, and the *day_of_training* variable is computed from how many of the training session come before.\n   218\t\n   219\t## 4-b.
